\left|\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}U_{h}^{\delta}(t)\right|=\frac{|\Gamma_{h}(0)|}{|\Gamma_{h}(t)|}\left|\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{h}(0)}U_{h,0}\right|<(1-\xi)\frac{|\Gamma_{h}(0)|}{|\Gamma_{h}(t)|}\leq 1-\xi,

for some
\xi>0
.
Here we have used the fact that
|\Gamma_{h}(0)|\leq|\Gamma_{h}(t)|
since
\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}\cdot V_{h}\geq 0
.
The second condition holds for
\delta
sufficiently small by using (
4.14
).

Proof of Lemma
4.7
, part 2
.

We note that as our initial data is admissible there exists
\xi>0
such that

\left|\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}U_{h}^{\delta}\right|=\frac{1}{|\Gamma_{h}(t)|}\left|\int_{\Gamma_{h}(0)}U_{h,0}\right|\leq\frac{1}{|\Gamma_{h}(0)|}\left|\int_{\Gamma_{h}(0)}U_{h,0}\right|<1-\xi.

Now by taking
\delta
sufficiently small we may use Lemma
4.8
to find that

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}I_{h}f^{\delta}(U_{h}^{\delta})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{|\Gamma_{h}(t)|}\int_{\left\{U_{h}^{\delta}(t)\leq 1-\frac{\xi}{2}\right\}}I_{h}f^{\delta}(U_{h}^{\delta})+\frac{1}{|\Gamma_{h}(t)|}\int_{\left\{U_{h}^{\delta}(t)>1-\frac{\xi}{2}\right\}}I_{h}f^{\delta}(U_{h}^{\delta})

\displaystyle\leq f^{\delta}\left(1-\frac{\xi}{2}\right)+\frac{\left(1-\frac{\xi}{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|\Gamma_{h}(t)|^{\frac{1}{2}}}\|I_{h}f^{\delta}(U_{h}^{\delta})\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}.

One can similarly show that

\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}I_{h}f^{\delta}(U_{h}^{\delta})\geq-f^{\delta}\left(1-\frac{\xi}{2}\right)-\frac{\left(1-\frac{\xi}{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|\Gamma_{h}(t)|^{\frac{1}{2}}}\|I_{h}f^{\delta}(U_{h}^{\delta})\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}.

Now by combining these bounds we observe

\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}I_{h}f^{\delta}(U_{h}^{\delta})\right)^{2}\leq\left(f^{\delta}\left(1-\frac{\xi}{2}\right)+\frac{\left(1-\frac{\xi}{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|\Gamma_{h}|^{\frac{1}{2}}}\|I_{h}f^{\delta}(U_{h}^{\delta})\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}\right)^{2}.

Noting that, for
a,b\in\mathbb{R}
,
(a+b)^{2}\leq a^{2}\left(1+\frac{2}{\xi}\right)+b^{2}\left(1+\frac{\xi}{2}\right)
one obtains

(4.17)

\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}I_{h}f^{\delta}(U_{h}^{\delta})\right)^{2}\leq\left(1+\frac{2}{\xi}\right)f^{\delta}\left(1-\frac{\xi}{2}\right)^{2}+\left(\frac{4-\xi^{2}}{4|\Gamma_{h}(t)|}\right)\|I_{h}f^{\delta}(U_{h}^{\delta})\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}^{2}.

Clearly we have that

\frac{1}{|\Gamma_{h}(t)|}\left\|I_{h}f^{\delta}(U_{h}^{\delta})-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}I_{h}f^{\delta}(U_{h}^{\delta})\right\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}^{2}=\frac{1}{|\Gamma_{h}(t)|}\|I_{h}f^{\delta}(U_{h}^{\delta})\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}^{2}-\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}I_{h}f^{\delta}(U_{h}^{\delta})\right)^{2},

and hence using (
4.17
),

(4.18)

\frac{\xi^{2}}{4|\Gamma_{h}(t)|}\|I_{h}f^{\delta}(U_{h}^{\delta})\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{|\Gamma_{h}(t)|}\left\|I_{h}f^{\delta}(U_{h}^{\delta})-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}I_{h}f^{\delta}(U_{h}^{\delta})\right\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}^{2}+\left(1+\frac{2}{\xi}\right)f^{\delta}\left(1-\frac{\xi}{2}\right)^{2}.

Now by construction, we can take
\delta
to be sufficiently small so that
f^{\delta}\left(1-\frac{\xi}{2}\right)=f\left(1-\frac{\xi}{2}\right)
, which removes the dependence on
\delta
.
The result readily follows by integrating (
4.18
) over
[0,T]
and using (
4.15
) gives (
4.16
).
∎

It is a straightforward application of the Poincaré inequality to see that our established bounds imply a bound

\int_{0}^{T}\|W_{h}^{\delta}\|_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}^{2}\leq C,

where
C
is independent of
\delta,h
.
This follows by obtaining a uniform bound on
\int_{0}^{T}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}W_{h}^{\delta}\right)^{2}
by testing (
4.5
) with
\phi_{h}=1
, and using (
4.6
).

4.2.2.
Passage to the limit

As a consequence of the established uniform bounds there exist functions
U_{h}\in L^{\infty}_{H^{1}},\partial^{\bullet}_{h}U_{h}\in L^{2}_{H^{-1}},W_{h}\in L^{2}_{H^{1}},\Phi_{h}\in L^{2}_{L^{2}}
, such that as (up to a subsequence of)
\delta\searrow 0
we have

\displaystyle U_{h}^{\delta}\overset{*}{\rightharpoonup}U_{h}\text{ weak-}*\text{ in }L^{\infty}_{H^{1}}\qquad\partial^{\bullet}_{h}{U_{h}^{\delta}}\rightharpoonup\partial^{\bullet}_{h}{U_{h}}\text{ weakly in }L^{2}_{H^{-1}},

\displaystyle W_{h}^{\delta}\rightharpoonup W_{h}\text{ weakly in }L^{2}_{H^{1}},\qquad I_{h}f^{\delta}\left(U_{h}^{\delta}\right)\rightharpoonup\Phi_{h}\text{ weakly in }L^{2}_{L^{2}},

with
U_{h}\in S_{h}^{T}
and
W_{h},\Phi_{h}\in\tilde{S}_{h}^{T}
.
Moreover the Aubin-Lions type result of
[
3
]
allows one to obtain a strongly convergent subsequence
U_{h}^{\delta}\rightarrow U_{h}
in
L^{2}_{L^{2}}
.

We now show that
|U_{h}|<1
a.e. on
\Gamma_{h}(t)
(for almost all
t\in[0,T]
), and
\Phi_{h}=I_{h}f(U_{h})
.
As a result of Lemma
4.6
, taking
\delta\rightarrow 0
we obtain the following.

Corollary 4.9
.

For almost all
t\in[0,T]
we have that
|U_{h}(t)|\leq 1
almost everywhere on
\Gamma_{h}(t)
.

Due to the poles of
f
at
\pm 1
, we need to strengthen this result to make sure that the set of values where
|U_{h}(t)|=1
has measure zero.
We note that since
U_{h}
is piecewise linear it is sufficient to show that we have
|U_{h}(x_{i}(t),t)|<1
for the nodes
(x_{i}(t))_{i=1,...,N_{h}}\subset\Gamma_{h}(t)
and almost all
t\in[0,T]
.
By the strong convergence
U_{h}^{\delta}\rightarrow U_{h}
in
L^{2}_{L^{2}}
we find that for almost all
x\in\Gamma_{h}(t)
and almost all
t\in[0,T]
that

\lim_{\delta\searrow 0}U_{h}^{\delta}(x,t)=U_{h}(x,t).

We show that this in fact holds for all
x\in\Gamma_{h}(t)
.

Lemma 4.10
.

Let
t\in[0,T]
be such that

\lim_{\delta\searrow 0}U_{h}^{\delta}(x,t)=U_{h}(x,t)

for almost all
x\in\Gamma_{h}(t)
.
Then this holds for all
x\in\Gamma_{h}(t)
.

Proof.

We fix
t
such that the assumption holds.
A straightforward application of the triangle inequality shows that pointwise convergence at the nodes of
\Gamma_{h}(t)
implies pointwise convergence everywhere.
Hence we verify that pointwise convergence holds at all of the nodes.
For a given node
x_{i}\in\Gamma_{h}(t)
of the triangulation
\mathcal{T}_{h}(t)
one has that

\left|U_{h}^{\delta}(x_{i})-U_{h}(x_{i})\right|\leq\left\|U_{h}^{\delta}-U_{h}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}\leq Ch^{-2}\left\|U_{h}^{\delta}-U_{h}\right\|_{L^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))},

where we have used an inverse inequality.
Since
U_{h}^{\delta}
is uniformly bounded (independent of
\delta
) due to (
4.6
) one may apply the dominated convergence theorem so that pointwise convergence almost everywhere implies convergence in
L^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))
from which the result follows.
∎

This is a necessary prerequisite for us to adapt an argument from
[
14
]
.

Lemma 4.11
.

For almost all
t\in[0,T]
one has that
|U_{h}(x,t)|<1
everywhere on
\Gamma_{h}(t)
.

Proof.

Firstly, we note that one can argue along the same lines as in
[
14
, Lemma 5.10]
to show the following.
Up to a subsequence of
\delta\searrow 0
we have for all
x\in\Gamma_{h}(t)
, and almost all
t\in[0,T]

(4.19)

\displaystyle\lim_{\delta\searrow 0}I_{h}f^{\delta}\left(U_{h}^{\delta}(x,t)\right)=\begin{cases}I_{h}f(U_{h}(x,t)),&\text{ if }|U_{h}(x,t)|<1\\
\infty,&\text{ otherwise}\end{cases}.

We note that for our purposes this requires the convergence on all of
\Gamma_{h}(t)
as discussed in the previous lemma.
Then we recall the bound (
4.16
), and use an inverse inequality to see that

\int_{0}^{T}\|I_{h}f^{\delta}\left(U_{h}^{\delta}(t)\right)\|_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}^{2}\leq Ch^{-2}\int_{0}^{T}\|I_{h}f^{\delta}\left(U_{h}^{\delta}(t)\right)\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}^{2}\leq Ch^{-2},

where the constant
C
is independent of
\delta
and
h
.
Taking the
\liminf
in
\delta
of the above and using Fatou’s lemma we find that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\liminf_{\delta\searrow 0}|f^{\delta}\left(U_{h}^{\delta}(x_{i},t)\right)|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T}\liminf_{\delta\searrow 0}\|I_{h}f^{\delta}\left(U_{h}^{\delta}(t)\right)\|_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\liminf_{\delta\searrow 0}\int_{0}^{T}\|I_{h}f^{\delta}\left(U_{h}^{\delta}(t)\right)\|_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}^{2}\leq Ch^{-2},

for all the nodes
x_{i}\in\Gamma_{h}(t)
.
Hence we have

\liminf_{\delta\searrow 0}|f^{\delta}\left(U_{h}^{\delta}(x_{i},t)\right)|<\infty,

for almost all
t\in[0,T]
, and hence by (
4.19
) that
|U_{h}(x_{i},t)|<1
for all nodes
x_{i}
.
Since this holds for all of the nodes, it holds for all
x\in\Gamma_{h}(t)
as
U_{h}
is piecewise linear.
∎

As a consequence of this result, we also find that as
\Phi_{h}=I_{h}f(U_{h})
.
Thus a solution pair
(U_{h}^{\delta},W_{h}^{\delta})
solving (
4.4
), (
4.5
) yields a solution pair
(U_{h},W_{h})
solving (
4.2
), (
4.3
) as
\delta\searrow 0
.
We note that lower semi-continuity of the norm implies a bound of the form

(4.20)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\|I_{h}f(U_{h})\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}^{2}\leq C,

for a constant
C
independent of
h
.

Lastly state a result showing continuous dependence on the initial data, from which we obtain uniqueness.

Proposition 4.12
.

Let
U^{1}_{h,0},U^{2}_{h,0}\in\mathcal{I}_{h,0}
be admissible initial data such that
\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.98003pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.26338pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.6363pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.45924pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{h}(0)}U^{1}_{h,0}=\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.98003pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.26338pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.6363pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.45924pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{h}(0)}U^{2}_{h,0}
.
Denoting the corresponding solution pairs as
(U^{i}_{h},W_{h}^{i})
with initial data
U_{h}^{i}(0)=U^{i}_{h,0}
we have

\left\|U^{1}_{h}(t)-U^{2}_{h}(t)\right\|_{-h}^{2}+\varepsilon\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}(U_{h}^{1}-U_{h}^{2})\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}^{2}\leq C\left\|U^{1}_{h,0}-U^{2}_{h,0}\right\|_{-h}^{2},

where
C
is independent of
h
and depends exponentially on
t
.

Proof.

We omit this proof as it is essentially identical to
[
14
, Proposition 4.12]
, where the only meaningful difference is we must use
\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}
rather than
\mathcal{G}
as in
[
14
]
(see Appendix
A
for the definition of these operators).
∎

4.3.
Error analysis

In this section we consider the error analysis of the pair
(U_{h},W_{h})
, with initial data
U_{h,0}=\Pi_{h}u_{0}
, where
u_{0}\in\mathcal{I}_{0}\cap H^{2}(\Gamma_{0})
.
We now show that
U_{h,0}\in\mathcal{I}_{h,0}
and so the preceding theory holds true.

Lemma 4.13
.

Let
u_{0}\in\mathcal{I}_{0}\cap H^{2}(\Gamma_{0})
be such that
|u_{0}|<1
.
Then, for sufficiently small
h
,
\Pi_{h}u_{0}\in\mathcal{I}_{h,0}
.

Proof.

By definition we have that

\left|\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{h}(0)}\Pi_{h}u_{0}\right|=\frac{|\Gamma_{0}|}{|\Gamma_{h}(0)|}\left|\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{0}}u_{0}\right|\leq(1-\xi)\frac{|\Gamma_{0}|}{|\Gamma_{h}(0)|}<1,

for sufficiently small
h
.
Similarly by using an inverse inequality and (
2.17
), (
2.20
) one finds

\displaystyle\|\Pi_{h}u_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{h}(0))}

\displaystyle\leq\|I_{h}u_{0}^{-\ell}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{h}(0))}+\|\Pi_{h}u_{0}-I_{h}u_{0}^{-\ell}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{h}(0))}

\displaystyle<1-\xi+Ch^{-1}\|\Pi_{h}u_{0}-I_{h}u_{0}^{-\ell}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(0))}

\displaystyle<1-\xi+Ch\|u_{0}\|_{H^{2}(\Gamma_{0})}<1.

With this we now find that

\displaystyle E^{\text{CH}}_{h}[\Pi_{h}u_{0}]=\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}\frac{\varepsilon|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}\Pi_{h}u_{0}|^{2}}{2}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}I_{h}F(\Pi_{h}u_{0})\leq C+C\|u_{0}\|_{H^{1}(\Gamma(t))}^{2},

where we have used (
2.14
), and the boundedness of
F
over
[-1,1]
.
∎

We adapt ideas from
[
7
,
8
]
to show an error bound for the semi-discrete scheme (
4.2
), (
4.3
).
Here
u^{\delta}
is the solution of the regularised continuous problem, as given as in the proof of (
3.7
).
For the error analysis, we require that
u^{\delta}
is such that

(4.21)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\left[\|u^{\delta}\|_{H^{2}(\Gamma(t))}^{2}+\|w^{\delta}\|_{H^{1}(\Gamma(t))}^{2}+\|f^{\delta}(u^{\delta})\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma(t))}^{2}+\|\partial^{\bullet}u^{\delta}\|_{H^{2}(\Gamma(t))}^{2}\right]<C,

for some constant
C
independent of
\delta
.
All of these bounds are known to hold, except for the derivative bound, which is more problematic.
A derivative bound of this form is assumed in
[
11
,
32
,
35
]
for the error analysis of ESFEM schemes for the Cahn-Hilliard equation with a regular potential.

To show an error bound, we decompose the error as

u^{-\ell}-U_{h}=[u^{-\ell}-(u^{\delta})^{-\ell}]+[(u^{\delta})^{-\ell}-U_{h}^{\delta}]+[U_{h}^{\delta}-U_{h}],

where the first term is bounded by using lifts and (
3.7
), and the third term is bounded similarly as in the following theorem.

Theorem 4.14
.

Let
(U_{h}^{\delta},W_{h}^{\delta})
be the unique solution of (
4.4
), (
4.5
), and
(U_{h},W_{h})
be the unique solution of (
4.2
), (
4.3
).
Then for sufficiently small
\delta>0
we have that

(4.22)

\displaystyle\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}(U_{h}-U_{h}^{\delta})\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}^{2}+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\|U_{h}-U_{h}^{\delta}\right\|_{-h}^{2}\leq C\delta,

for
C
a constant independent of
\delta,h
.

Proof.

This bound follows almost identically to (
3.7
), where we use (
4.20
) to bound the potential term.
We omit the details.
∎

Thus the main goal of this subsection is bounding the error for the regularised terms,
(u^{\delta})^{-\ell}-U_{h}^{\delta}
.
We consider the same idea as in
[
32
]
, and decompose the error as

(u^{\delta})^{-\ell}-U_{h}^{\delta}=\underbrace{(u^{\delta})^{-\ell}-\Pi_{h}u^{\delta}}_{=:\rho_{u}^{\delta}}+\underbrace{\Pi_{h}u^{\delta}-U_{h}^{\delta}}_{=:\sigma_{u}^{\delta}},\quad(w^{\delta})^{-\ell}-W_{h}^{\delta}=\underbrace{(w^{\delta})^{-\ell}-\Pi_{h}w^{\delta}}_{=:\rho_{w}^{\delta}}+\underbrace{\Pi_{h}w^{\delta}-W_{h}^{\delta}}_{=:\sigma_{u}^{\delta}}.

We observe, from our assumption (
4.21
), that by applying (
2.17
) we find

(4.23)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\|\rho_{u}^{\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}^{2}+h^{2}\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}\rho_{u}^{\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}^{2}\leq Ch^{4},

for a constant
C
independent of
\delta,h
, and hence it only remains to bound
\sigma_{u}^{\delta}
.

We observe that by using both versions of transport theorem

\displaystyle m\left(\partial^{\bullet}{u^{\delta}},\phi_{h}^{\ell}\right)+g(u^{\delta},\phi_{h}^{\ell})

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{dt}m(u^{\delta},\phi_{h}^{\ell})-m(u^{\delta},\partial^{\bullet}\phi_{h}^{\ell})

\displaystyle=m(\partial^{\bullet}_{\ell}u^{\delta},\phi_{h}^{\ell})+g_{\ell}(u^{\delta},\phi_{h}^{\ell})+m(u^{\delta},\partial^{\bullet}_{\ell}\phi_{h}^{\ell}-\partial^{\bullet}\phi_{h}^{\ell})

and hence
u^{\delta},w^{\delta}
solve

\displaystyle m\left(\partial^{\bullet}_{\ell}{u^{\delta}},\phi_{h}^{\ell}\right)+g_{\ell}(u^{\delta},\phi_{h}^{\ell})+a(w^{\delta},\phi_{h}^{\ell})=m(u^{\delta},\partial^{\bullet}\phi_{h}^{\ell}-\partial^{\bullet}_{\ell}\phi_{h}^{\ell}),

\displaystyle m(w^{\delta},\phi_{h}^{\ell})=\varepsilon a(u^{\delta},\phi_{h}^{\ell})+\frac{\theta}{2\varepsilon}m(f^{\delta}(u^{\delta}),\phi_{h}^{\ell})-\frac{1}{\varepsilon}m(u^{\delta},\phi_{h}^{\ell}).

Now as
U_{h}^{\delta},W_{h}^{\delta}
solve (
4.4
), (
4.5
) one obtains, using the definition of the Ritz projection that
\sigma_{u}^{\delta},\sigma_{w}^{\delta}
solve

(4.24)

\displaystyle\bar{m}_{h}(\partial^{\bullet}_{h}\sigma_{u}^{\delta},\phi_{h})+g_{h}(I_{h}(\sigma_{u}^{\delta}\phi_{h}),1)+a_{h}(\sigma_{w}^{\delta},\phi_{h})=\sum_{i=1}^{3}E_{i}(\phi_{h}),

(4.25)

\displaystyle\bar{m}_{h}(\sigma_{w}^{\delta},\phi_{h})=\varepsilon a_{h}(\sigma_{u}^{\delta},\phi_{h})+\bar{m}_{h}(I_{h}f^{\delta}(\Pi_{h}u^{\delta})-I_{h}f^{\delta}(U_{h}^{\delta}),\phi_{h})-\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\bar{m}_{h}(\sigma_{u}^{\delta},\phi_{h})+\sum_{i=4}^{6}E_{i}(\phi_{h}),

where
E_{i}(\phi_{h})
denote some consistency errors, given by

\displaystyle E_{1}(\phi_{h}):=\bar{m}_{h}(\partial^{\bullet}_{h}\Pi_{h}u^{\delta},\phi_{h})-m(\partial^{\bullet}u^{\delta},\phi_{h}^{\ell}),

\displaystyle E_{2}(\phi_{h}):=g_{h}(I_{h}(\Pi_{h}u^{\delta}\phi_{h}),1)-g_{\ell}(u^{\delta},\phi_{h}^{\ell}),

\displaystyle E_{3}(\phi_{h}):=m(u^{\delta},\partial^{\bullet}\phi_{h}^{\ell}-\partial^{\bullet}_{\ell}\phi_{h}^{\ell}),

\displaystyle E_{4}(\phi_{h}):=\bar{m}_{h}(\Pi_{h}w^{\delta},\phi_{h})-m(w^{\delta},\phi_{h}^{\ell}),

\displaystyle E_{5}(\phi_{h}):=\frac{\theta}{2\varepsilon}\left[m(f^{\delta}(u^{\delta}),\phi_{h}^{\ell})-\bar{m}_{h}(I_{h}f^{\delta}(\Pi_{h}u^{\delta}),\phi_{h})\right],

\displaystyle E_{6}(\phi_{h}):=\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\left[\bar{m}_{h}(\Pi_{h}u^{\delta},\phi_{h})-m(u^{\delta},\phi_{h}^{\ell})\right].

We will be interested in taking
\phi_{h}
to be
\sigma_{u}^{\delta}
or
\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\sigma_{u}^{\delta}
, where we note that the latter is well-defined
4
4

4

This follows as
\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}U_{h}^{\delta}=\int_{\Gamma_{h}(0)}\Pi_{h}u_{0}=\int_{\Gamma(0)}u_{0}=\int_{\Gamma(t)}u=\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}\Pi_{h}u
, by the mass-conserving properties of both the Ritz projection and solutions of the Cahn-Hilliard equation.
, and bounding the corresponding
E_{i}(\phi_{h})
.
This is the content of the following lemma.

Lemma 4.15
.

For
E_{1},...,E_{6}
defined as above we have

\displaystyle|E_{1}(\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\sigma_{u}^{\delta})|\leq Ch^{2}\left(\|u^{\delta}\|_{H^{2}(\Gamma(t))}+\|\partial^{\bullet}u^{\delta}\|_{H^{2}(\Gamma(t))}\right)\|\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\sigma_{u}^{\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))},

\displaystyle|E_{2}(\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\sigma_{u}^{\delta})|\leq Ch^{2}\|u^{\delta}\|_{H^{2}(\Gamma(t))}\|\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\sigma_{u}^{\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma(t))},

\displaystyle|E_{3}(\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\sigma_{u}^{\delta})|\leq Ch^{2}\|u^{\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma(t))}\left\|\sigma_{u}^{\delta}\right\|_{-h},

\displaystyle|E_{4}(\sigma_{u}^{\delta})|\leq Ch\|w^{\delta}\|_{H^{1}(\Gamma(t))}\|\sigma_{u}^{\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))},

\displaystyle|E_{5}(\sigma_{u}^{\delta})|\leq\left(\frac{Ch^{2}\log\left(\frac{1}{h}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\delta}\|u^{\delta}\|_{H^{2}(\Gamma(t))}+Ch^{2}\|f^{\delta}(u^{\delta})\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma(t))}\right)\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}\sigma_{u}^{\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))},

\displaystyle|E_{6}(\sigma_{u}^{\delta})|\leq Ch^{2}\|u^{\delta}\|_{H^{2}(\Gamma(t))}\|\nabla_{\Gamma}\sigma_{u}^{\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}.

Proof.

We begin by writing

\displaystyle E_{1}(\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\sigma_{u}^{\delta})

\displaystyle=\left[\bar{m}_{h}(\partial^{\bullet}_{h}\Pi_{h}u^{\delta},\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\sigma_{u}^{\delta})-m_{h}(\partial^{\bullet}_{h}\Pi_{h}u^{\delta},\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\sigma_{u}^{\delta})\right]

\displaystyle+\left[m_{h}(\partial^{\bullet}_{h}\Pi_{h}u^{\delta},\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\sigma_{u}^{\delta})-m(\partial^{\bullet}_{\ell}\pi_{h}u^{\delta},(\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\sigma_{u}^{\delta})^{\ell})\right]

\displaystyle+\left[m(\partial^{\bullet}_{\ell}(\pi_{h}u^{\delta}-u^{\delta}),(\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\sigma_{u}^{\delta})^{\ell})\right]+\left[m(\partial^{\bullet}_{\ell}u^{\delta}-\partial^{\bullet}u^{\delta},(\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\sigma_{u}^{\delta})^{\ell})\right],

and hence using (
2.5
), (
2.7
), (
2.18
), (
2.19
), (
2.26
), and (
2.2
) where necessary, one concludes the bounds for
|E_{1}(\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\sigma_{u}^{\delta})|
.

Next we write
E_{2}(\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\sigma_{u}^{\delta})
as

\displaystyle E_{2}(\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\sigma_{u}^{\delta})

\displaystyle=\left[g_{h}(I_{h}(\Pi_{h}u^{\delta}\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\sigma_{u}^{\delta}),1)-g_{h}(\Pi_{h}u^{\delta},\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\sigma_{u}^{\delta})\right]

\displaystyle+\left[g_{h}(\Pi_{h}u^{\delta},\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\sigma_{u}^{\delta})-g_{\ell}(\pi_{h}u^{\delta},(\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\sigma_{u}^{\delta})^{\ell})\right]+g_{\ell}(\pi_{h}u^{\delta}-u^{\delta},(\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\sigma_{u}^{\delta})^{\ell}),

from which the stated bound follows from (
2.9
), (
2.14
), (
2.17
) and (an appropriate analogue of) (
2.26
).
The bound for
E_{3}(\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\sigma_{u}^{\delta})
follows immediately from (
2.5
) and the Poincaré inequality.
Likewise the bound for
E_{4}(\sigma_{u}^{\delta})
follows by similar arguments to the above, using (
2.7
), (
2.15
), (
2.25
).
The last of the simple bounds is for
E_{6}(\sigma_{u}^{\delta})
, where we write

E_{6}(\sigma_{u}^{\delta})=\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\left[\bar{m}_{h}(\Pi_{h}u^{\delta},\sigma_{u}^{\delta})-m_{h}(\Pi_{h}u^{\delta},\sigma_{u}^{\delta})\right]+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\left[m_{h}(\Pi_{h}u^{\delta},\sigma_{u}^{\delta})-m(\pi_{h}u^{\delta},(\sigma_{u}^{\delta})^{\ell})\right]\\
+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}m(\pi_{h}u^{\delta}-u^{\delta},(\sigma_{u}^{\delta})^{\ell}),

and use (
2.7
), (
2.17
), (
2.26
).
We note here that since
\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}\sigma_{u}^{\delta}=0
, we can use the Poincaré inequality here so that
\|\sigma_{u}^{\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}\leq C\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}\sigma_{u}^{\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}
— we shall do this throughout.

The bound for
E_{5}(\sigma_{u}^{\delta})
naturally is the most complicated due to the singular potential.
We begin by bounding
E_{5}(\sigma_{u}^{\delta})
by

|E_{5}(\sigma_{u}^{\delta})|\leq\frac{\theta}{2\varepsilon}\left|\bar{m}_{h}(I_{h}f^{\delta}(\Pi_{h}u^{\delta}),\sigma_{u}^{\delta})-m_{h}(I_{h}f^{\delta}(\Pi_{h}u^{\delta}),\sigma_{u}^{\delta})\right|+\frac{\theta}{2\varepsilon}|m_{h}(I_{h}f^{\delta}(\Pi_{h}u^{\delta})-I_{h}f^{\delta}((u^{\delta})^{-\ell}),\sigma_{u}^{\delta})|\\
+\frac{\theta}{2\varepsilon}\left|m_{h}(I_{h}f^{\delta}((u^{\delta})^{-\ell}),\sigma_{u}^{\delta})-m(I_{h}^{\ell}f^{\delta}(u^{\delta}),(\sigma_{u}^{\delta})^{\ell})\right|+\frac{\theta}{2\varepsilon}|m(I_{h}^{\ell}f^{\delta}(u^{\delta})-f^{\delta}(u^{\delta}),(\sigma_{u}^{\delta})^{\ell})|.

Now by using (
2.26
) one finds

\left|\bar{m}_{h}(I_{h}f^{\delta}(\Pi_{h}u^{\delta}),\sigma_{u}^{\delta})-m_{h}(I_{h}f^{\delta}(\Pi_{h}u^{\delta}),\sigma_{u}^{\delta})\right|\leq Ch^{2}\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}I_{h}f^{\delta}(\Pi_{h}u^{\delta})\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}\sigma_{u}^{\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))},

and so it is apparent we need a bound for
\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}I_{h}f^{\delta}(\Pi_{h}u^{\delta})\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}
.
To do this we use (
2.23
) to see that

\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}I_{h}f^{\delta}(\Pi_{h}u^{\delta})\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}^{2}\leq\frac{C}{\delta}a_{h}(I_{h}f^{\delta}(\Pi_{h}u^{\delta}),\Pi_{h}u^{\delta}),

whereby Young’s inequality and (
2.14
) yields

(4.26)

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}I_{h}f^{\delta}(\Pi_{h}u^{\delta})\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}\leq\frac{C}{\delta}\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}\Pi_{h}u^{\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}\leq\frac{C}{\delta}\|u^{\delta}\|_{H^{1}(\Gamma(t))}.

Next we observe that

m_{h}(I_{h}f^{\delta}(\Pi_{h}u^{\delta})-I_{h}f^{\delta}((u^{\delta})^{-\ell}),\sigma_{u}^{\delta})=m_{h}(I_{h}f^{\delta}(\Pi_{h}u^{\delta})-I_{h}f^{\delta}(I_{h}(u^{\delta})^{-\ell}),\sigma_{u}^{\delta})

and it is straightforward to see that, by using
|(f^{\delta})^{\prime}(r)|\leq\frac{C}{\delta}

\displaystyle\|I_{h}f^{\delta}(\Pi_{h}u^{\delta})-I_{h}f^{\delta}(I_{h}(u^{\delta})^{-\ell})\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma(t))}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|I_{h}f^{\delta}(\Pi_{h}u^{\delta})-I_{h}f^{\delta}(I_{h}(u^{\delta})^{-\ell})\right\|_{h,t}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\delta}\|\Pi_{h}u^{\delta}-I_{h}(u^{\delta})^{-\ell}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma(t))}\leq\frac{Ch^{2}}{\delta}\|u^{\delta}\|_{H^{2}(\Gamma(t))},

where we have used (
2.17
), (
2.20
) in the final inequality.
From (
2.2
) and (
2.7
) one finds

|m_{h}(I_{h}f^{\delta}((u^{\delta})^{-\ell}),\sigma_{u}^{\delta})-m(I_{h}^{\ell}f^{\delta}(u^{\delta}),(\sigma_{u}^{\delta})^{\ell})|\leq Ch^{2}\|I_{h}^{\ell}f^{\delta}(u^{\delta})\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma(t))}\|\sigma_{u}^{\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))},

where we will bound
\|I_{h}^{\ell}f^{\delta}(u^{\delta})\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma(t))}
later.
For the final term we use an argument from
[
8
,
10
]
.
From Hölder’s inequality we find

|m(I_{h}^{\ell}f^{\delta}(u^{\delta})-f^{\delta}(u^{\delta}),(\sigma_{u}^{\delta})^{\ell})|\leq C\|I_{h}^{\ell}f^{\delta}(u^{\delta})-f^{\delta}(u^{\delta})\|_{L^{1}(\Gamma(t))}\|\sigma_{u}^{\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}.

Now by following an argument in the proof of
[
8
, Theorem 3.2]
one can show that
f^{\delta}(u^{\delta})\in H^{2,1}(\Gamma(t))
such that

\|f^{\delta}(u^{\delta})\|_{H^{2,1}(\Gamma(t))}\leq\frac{C}{\delta}\|u^{\delta}\|_{H^{2,1}(\Gamma(t))}\leq\frac{C}{\delta}\|u^{\delta}\|_{H^{2}(\Gamma(t))}.

Thus using (
2.4
) and (
2.21
) one finds that

\|I_{h}^{\ell}f^{\delta}(u^{\delta})-f^{\delta}(u^{\delta})\|_{L^{1}(\Gamma(t))}\|\sigma_{u}^{\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}\leq\frac{Ch^{2}}{\delta}\log\left(\frac{1}{h}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\|u^{\delta}\|_{H^{2}(\Gamma(t))}\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}\sigma_{u}^{\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}.

The final thing to note now is that by using (
2.21
), and the above bound for
\|f^{\delta}(u^{\delta})\|_{H^{2,1}(\Gamma(t))}
, it is clear that

\|I_{h}^{\ell}f^{\delta}(u^{\delta})\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma(t))}\leq C\|f^{\delta}(u^{\delta})\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma(t))}+\frac{Ch}{\delta}\|u^{\delta}\|_{H^{2}(\Gamma(t))}.

Piecing this together yields the bound for
E_{5}(\sigma_{u}^{\delta})
.
∎

Theorem 4.16
.

For
\sigma_{u}^{\delta}
defined as above, and
\delta
sufficiently small, then assuming (
4.21
) holds we have

(4.27)

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\|\sigma_{u}^{\delta}\right\|_{-h}^{2}+\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}\sigma_{u}^{\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}^{2}\leq C\left(h^{2}+\frac{h^{4}\log\left(\frac{1}{h}\right)}{\delta^{2}}\right),

where
C
depends on
\varepsilon,T,\theta,u_{0}
.

Proof.

We test (
4.24
) with
\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\sigma_{u}^{\delta}
and (
4.25
) with
\sigma_{u}^{\delta}
to see that

\bar{m}_{h}(\partial^{\bullet}_{h}\sigma_{u}^{\delta},\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\sigma_{u}^{\delta})+g_{h}(I_{h}(\sigma_{u}^{\delta}\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\sigma_{u}^{\delta}),1)+\varepsilon a_{h}(\sigma_{u}^{\delta},\sigma_{u}^{\delta})+\bar{m}_{h}(I_{h}f^{\delta}(\Pi_{h}u^{\delta})-I_{h}f^{\delta}(U_{h}^{\delta}),\sigma_{u}^{\delta})\\
=\sum_{i=1}^{3}E_{i}(\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\sigma_{u}^{\delta})-\sum_{i=4}^{6}E_{i}(\sigma_{u}^{\delta})+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\bar{m}_{h}(\sigma_{u}^{\delta},\sigma_{u}^{\delta}).

One then writes (using Proposition
2.17
and Lemma
2.29
)

\displaystyle\bar{m}_{h}(\partial^{\bullet}_{h}\sigma_{u}^{\delta},\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\sigma_{u}^{\delta})+g_{h}(I_{h}(\sigma_{u}^{\delta}\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\sigma_{u}^{\delta}),1)

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{dt}\bar{m}_{h}(\sigma_{u}^{\delta},\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\sigma_{u}^{\delta})-\bar{m}_{h}(\sigma_{u}^{\delta},\partial^{\bullet}_{h}\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\sigma_{u}^{\delta})

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\sigma_{u}^{\delta}\right\|_{-h}^{2}-a_{h}(\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\sigma_{u}^{\delta},\partial^{\bullet}_{h}\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\sigma_{u}^{\delta})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\sigma_{u}^{\delta}\right\|_{-h}^{2}-\frac{1}{2}b_{h}(\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\sigma_{u}^{\delta},\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\sigma_{u}^{\delta}).

Now using this, and the monotonicity of
f^{\delta}(\cdot)
, in the above we find

(4.28)

\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\sigma_{u}^{\delta}\right\|_{-h}^{2}+\varepsilon\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}\sigma_{u}^{\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}^{2}\\
\leq\frac{1}{2}b_{h}(\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\sigma_{u}^{\delta},\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\sigma_{u}^{\delta})+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\left\|\sigma_{u}^{\delta}\right\|_{h,t}^{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{3}|E_{i}(\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\sigma_{u}^{\delta})|+\sum_{i=4}^{6}|E_{i}(\sigma_{u}^{\delta})|.

We note that from the smoothness of
V
and (
2.24
) one can show

\frac{1}{2}b_{h}(\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\sigma_{u}^{\delta},\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\sigma_{u}^{\delta})+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\left\|\sigma_{u}^{\delta}\right\|_{h,t}^{2}\leq C\left\|\sigma_{u}^{\delta}\right\|_{-h}^{2}+\frac{\varepsilon}{8}\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}\sigma_{u}^{\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}^{2}.

All that is left is to use the bounds from Lemma
4.15
and Young/Poincaré inequalities where necessary to see that

\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\sigma_{u}^{\delta}\right\|_{-h}^{2}+\varepsilon\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}\sigma_{u}^{\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}^{2}\leq C\left\|\sigma_{u}^{\delta}\right\|_{-h}^{2}+\frac{Ch^{4}\log\left(\frac{1}{h}\right)}{\delta^{2}}\|u^{\delta}\|_{H^{2}(\Gamma(t))}^{2}\\
+Ch^{2}\|w^{\delta}\|_{H^{1}(\Gamma(t))}^{2}+Ch^{4}\left(\|u^{\delta}\|_{H^{2}(\Gamma(t))}^{2}+\|\partial^{\bullet}u^{\delta}\|_{H^{2}(\Gamma(t))}^{2}+\|f^{\delta}(u^{\delta})\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma(t))}^{2}\right),

whence the result follows by applying the Grönwall inequality — noting that
\sigma_{u}^{\delta}(0)=0
, and that we have used our assumption (
4.21
).
∎

Remark 4.17
.

Inspecting this proof one finds that this still holds under the weaker assumption that

\int_{0}^{T}\|\partial^{\bullet}u^{\delta}\|_{H^{2}(\Gamma(t))}^{2}\leq\frac{C}{\delta^{2}},

which is more likely to hold, as the non-regularised problem is known to have limited regularity properties and hence a bound on
\partial^{\bullet}u^{\delta}
is likely to degenerate as
\delta\rightarrow 0
.

Now that we have bounded all of the relevant terms, we obtain an error bound for the semi-discrete scheme.

Theorem 4.18
.

Let
(u,w)
be the unique solution of (
3.1
), (
3.2
) with initial data
u_{0}\in\mathcal{I}_{0}\cap H^{2}(\Gamma_{0})
and
\|u_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{0})}<1
.
Let
(U_{h},W_{h})
the unique solution of (
4.2
), (
4.3
) with initial data
U_{h,0}=\Pi_{h}u_{0}
.
Then for sufficiently small
h
,

(4.29)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\varepsilon\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}(u^{-\ell}-U_{h})\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}^{2}\leq Ch^{\frac{4}{3}}\log\left(\frac{1}{h}\right).

Proof.

By combining (
3.7
), (
4.22
), (
4.23
), (
4.27
) we find that

\int_{0}^{T}\varepsilon\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}(u^{-\ell}-U_{h})\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}^{2}\leq C\left(\delta+h^{2}+\frac{h^{4}\log\left(\frac{1}{h}\right)}{\delta^{2}}\right).

Now we choose
\delta=C(p)h^{p}
for some value
p
to be determined and some constant
C(p)
dependent on
p
so that
\delta
is sufficiently small.
The optimal choice of
p
is then
p=\frac{4}{3}
and gives the result.
∎

Remark 4.19
.

(1)

We do not immediately obtain an
L^{\infty}_{H^{-1}}
error bound, as we have for the individual results (
3.7
), (
4.22
), (
4.27
).
As this requires some notion of how one can compare the norms
\|\cdot\|_{-1},\left\|\cdot\right\|_{-h}
.

(2)

From Proposition
4.12
is clear that this result holds for initial data
\tilde{U}_{h,0}\in\mathcal{I}_{h,0}
such that

\int_{\Gamma_{h}(0)}\tilde{U}_{h,0}=\int_{\Gamma_{0}}u_{0},\text{ and }\left\|\Pi_{h}u_{0}-\tilde{U}_{h,0}\right\|_{-h}\leq Ch^{\frac{2}{3}}\log\left(\frac{1}{h}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},

for some constant
C
independent of
h
.
A notable example of this is choosing
\tilde{U}_{h,0}=I_{h}u_{0}^{-\ell}-\tilde{c},

where

\tilde{c}=\frac{1}{|\Gamma_{h}(0)|}\left(\int_{\Gamma_{h}(0)}I_{h}u_{0}^{-\ell}-\int_{\Gamma_{0}}u_{0}\right).

It is straightforward to show that
\tilde{U}_{h,0}\in\mathcal{I}_{h,0}
, and that
|\tilde{c}|=\mathcal{O}(h^{2})
.
This justifies our choice of using the interpolant as initial data in our numerical experiments — as for sufficiently small
h
this term is negligible.

5.
Full discretisation of the problem

5.1.
Time discretisation

We consider a backward Euler time discretisation, with timestep
\tau>0
, of the system (
4.2
), (
4.3
), introducing further numerical integration terms, to give our fully discrete scheme.

Notation 5.1
.

(1)

We use the notation
t_{n}:=n\tau
,
\Gamma_{h}^{n}:=\Gamma_{h}(t_{n})
,
S_{h}^{n}:=S_{h}(t_{n})
.

(2)

For functions
\phi_{h}^{n-1}\in S_{h}^{n-1},\phi_{h}^{n}\in S_{h}^{n}
we define
\overline{\phi_{h}^{n-1}}\in S_{h}^{n}
and
\underline{\phi_{h}^{n}}\in S_{h}^{n-1}
to be the functions with the same nodal values, but on the succeeding/preceding surfaces respectively.

(3)

We define a fully discrete material time derivative for a sequence of functions
(\phi_{h}^{n})_{n}
for
\phi_{h}^{n}\in S_{h}^{n}
by

\partial^{\bullet}_{\tau}\phi_{h}^{n}:=\frac{1}{\tau}\left(\phi_{h}^{n}-\overline{\phi_{h}^{n-1}}\right)\in S_{h}^{n}.

For simplicity we assume that
N_{T}:=\frac{T}{\tau}\in\mathbb{N}
— the analysis in this section can be modified to include non-uniform timestep sizes and timestep sizes such that
N_{T}:=\lfloor\frac{T}{\tau}\rfloor\neq\frac{T}{\tau}
, but we shall not consider this here.
With this notation we now pose our fully discrete scheme.
We let
U_{h,0}\in\mathcal{I}_{h,0}
be an approximation for some
u_{0}\in\mathcal{I}_{0}
, as in the semi-discrete case.
Then for
n\geq 1
and data
(U_{h}^{n-1},W_{h}^{n-1})
we want to find
(U_{h}^{n},W_{h}^{n})\in S_{h}^{n}\times S_{h}^{n}
such that

(5.1)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\tau}\left(\bar{m}_{h}(t_{n};U^{n}_{h},\phi_{h}^{n})-\bar{m}_{h}(t_{n-1};U_{h}^{n-1},\phi_{h}^{n-1})\right)+a_{h}(t_{n};W_{h}^{n},\phi_{h}^{n})=\bar{m}_{h}(t_{n-1};U_{h}^{n-1},\underline{\partial^{\bullet}_{\tau}\phi_{h}^{n}}),

(5.2)

\displaystyle\bar{m}_{h}(t_{n};W_{h}^{n},\phi_{h}^{n})=\varepsilon a_{h}(t_{n};U^{n}_{h},\phi_{h}^{n})+\frac{\theta}{2\varepsilon}\bar{m}_{h}(t_{n};I_{h}f(U^{n}_{h}),\phi_{h}^{n})-\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\bar{m}_{h}(t_{n};U^{n}_{h},\phi_{h}^{n}),

for all
\phi_{h}^{n-1}\in S_{h}^{n-1},\phi_{h}^{n}\in S_{h}^{n}
, and such that
U_{h}^{0}=U_{h,0}
.
As in
[
35
]
, we note that in our notation we can write (
5.1
) as

\frac{1}{\tau}\left(\bar{m}_{h}(t_{n};U^{n}_{h},\phi_{h}^{n})-\bar{m}_{h}(t_{n-1};U_{h}^{n-1},\underline{\phi_{h}^{n}})\right)+a_{h}(W_{h}^{n},\phi_{h}^{n})=0,

which is a more natural formulation of this equality.
This form will be used throughout, notably in proving existence by a similar argument to that in
[
35
]
.
From here on we shall omit the time argument from the bilinear forms, as the timestep will be clear from context.

5.2.
Well-posedness

5.2.1.
Regularisation

As before, we show well-posedness by considering the regularised potential (
3.3
) and the corresponding equations.
As such we are interested in the following regularised problem for
\delta\in(0,1)
.

Given initial data
U_{h,0}\in\mathcal{I}_{h,0}
, approximating some
u_{0}\in\mathcal{I}_{0}
, we want to find
(U_{h}^{n,\delta},W_{h}^{n,\delta})\in S_{h}^{n}\times S_{h}^{n}
such that

(5.3)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\tau}\left(\bar{m}_{h}(U_{h}^{n,\delta},\phi_{h}^{n})-\bar{m}_{h}(U_{h}^{n-1,\delta},\underline{\phi_{h}^{n}})\right)+a_{h}(W_{h}^{n,\delta},\phi_{h}^{n})=0,

(5.4)

\displaystyle\bar{m}_{h}(W_{h}^{n,\delta},\phi_{h}^{n})=\varepsilon a_{h}(U_{h}^{n,\delta},\phi_{h}^{n})+\frac{\theta}{2\varepsilon}\bar{m}_{h}(I_{h}f^{\delta}(U_{h}^{n,\delta}),\phi_{h}^{n})-\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\bar{m}_{h}(U_{h}^{n,\delta},\phi_{h}^{n}),

for all
\phi_{h}^{n}\in S_{h}^{n}
, and such that
U_{h}^{0,\delta}=U_{h,0}
.

5.2.2.
Existence

To show existence, we consider the minimisation of an appropriate functional, as in
[
21
,
30
,
35
]
.
To do so we first state some results from
[
25
]
.

Lemma 5.2

(
[
25
, Lemma 3.6]
)
.

For
\phi_{h}^{n}\in S_{h}^{n}
,
\tau
sufficiently small and
t\in[t_{n-1},t_{n}]
there exists a constant
C
independent of
t,\tau,h
such that

(5.5)

\displaystyle\|\underline{\phi_{h}^{n}}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\phi_{h}^{n}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})},

(5.6)

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}\underline{\phi_{h}^{n}}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}\phi_{h}^{n}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})},

where
\underline{\phi_{h}^{n}}(t)
is the function on
\Gamma_{h}(t)
with the same nodal values as
\phi_{h}^{n}
.
In this notation
\underline{\phi_{h}^{n}}=\underline{\phi_{h}^{n}}(t_{n-1})
.

Lemma 5.3
.

For
\zeta_{h}^{n},\eta_{h}^{n}\in S_{h}^{n}
and sufficiently small
\tau
we have

(5.7)

\displaystyle|m_{h}(\zeta_{h}^{n},\eta_{h}^{n})-m_{h}(\underline{\zeta_{h}^{n}},\underline{\eta_{h}^{n}})|

\displaystyle\leq C\tau\|\zeta_{h}^{n}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}\|\eta_{h}^{n}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})},

(5.8)

\displaystyle|a_{h}(\zeta_{h}^{n},\eta_{h}^{n})-a_{h}(\underline{\zeta_{h}^{n}},\underline{\eta_{h}^{n}})|

\displaystyle\leq C\tau\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}\zeta_{h}^{n}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}\eta_{h}^{n}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})},

(5.9)

\displaystyle|\bar{m}_{h}(\zeta_{h}^{n},\eta_{h}^{n})-\bar{m}_{h}(\underline{\zeta_{h}^{n}},\underline{\eta_{h}^{n}})|

\displaystyle\leq C\tau\left\|\zeta_{h}^{n}\right\|_{h,t}\left\|\eta_{h}^{n}\right\|_{h,t},

where
C
denotes a constant independent of
\tau,h
.

Proof.

(
5.7
) is shown in
[
25
]
by combining Lemma 3.6 and Lemma 3.7, and the previous result.
The proofs for (
5.8
), (
5.9
) follow similarly.
∎

Corollary 5.4
.

Let
\tau
be sufficiently small, then for
\phi_{h}^{n}\in S_{h}^{n}
we have that

(5.10)

\displaystyle\|\phi_{h}^{n}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\underline{\phi_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n-1})},

(5.11)

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}\phi_{h}^{n}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}\underline{\phi_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n-1})},,

where
C
denotes a constant independent of
\tau,h
.

Proof.

For (
5.10
) this follows by writing

\|\phi_{h}^{n}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}^{2}=\|\underline{\phi_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n-1})}^{2}+\left[\|\phi_{h}^{n}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}^{2}-\|\underline{\phi_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n-1})}^{2}\right],

and bounding the term in square brackets by using (
5.7
) with a sufficiently small
\tau
.
(
5.11
) follows similarly.
∎

In fact these results can be generalised, where one replaces
\underline{\phi_{h}^{n}}\in S_{h}^{n}
with
\underline{\phi_{h}^{n}}(t)\in S_{h}(t)
for
t\in[t_{n-1},t_{n}]
.

Following the approach of
[
35
]
now introduce some tools to be used in showing existence of a solution.

Definition 5.5
.

For
z_{h}^{n-1}\in S_{h}^{n-1}
we define
z_{h,+}^{n-1}\in S_{h}^{n}
to be the unique solution of

(5.12)

\displaystyle\bar{m}_{h}(t_{n};z_{h,+}^{n-1},\phi_{h}^{n})=\bar{m}_{h}(t_{n-1};z_{h}^{n-1},\underline{\phi}_{h}^{n}),

for all
\phi_{h}^{n}\in S_{h}^{n}
.

This is clearly well defined by the Lax-Milgram theorem.
This time projection has the following properties.

Lemma 5.6
.

For
z_{h}^{n-1}\in S_{h}^{n-1}
and
z_{h,+}^{n-1}
as defined above we have:

(5.13)

\displaystyle\|z_{h,+}^{n-1}\|_{h,t_{n}}\leq C\|z_{h}^{n-1}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n-1})},

(5.14)

\displaystyle\|\overline{z_{h}^{n-1}}-z_{h,+}^{n-1}\|_{h,t_{n}}\leq C{\tau}\|z_{h}^{n-1}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n-1})},

where
C
denotes a constant independent of
\tau,h
.

Proof.

To show (
5.13
) we test (
5.12
) with
z_{h,+}^{n-1}
and use (
2.24
), (
5.5
) to see that

\|z_{h,+}^{n-1}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}^{2}\leq C\|\underline{z_{h,+}^{n-1}}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n-1})}\|z_{h}^{n-1}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n-1})}\leq C\|z_{h,+}^{n-1}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}\|z_{h}^{n-1}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n-1})},

from which (
5.13
) holds.
To show (
5.14
) we note that

\bar{m}_{h}(\overline{z_{h}^{n-1}}-z_{h,+}^{n-1},\phi_{h}^{n})=\bar{m}_{h}(\overline{z_{h}^{n-1}},\phi_{h}^{n})-\bar{m}_{h}(z_{h}^{n-1},\underline{\phi_{h}^{n}}),

and hence taking
\phi_{h}^{n}=\overline{z_{h}^{n-1}}-z_{h,+}^{n-1}
and using (
5.7
) one obtains the result.
∎

Using this time projection we notice that one may write (
5.3
) as

(5.15)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\tau}\bar{m}_{h}(U_{h}^{n,\delta}-U_{h,+}^{n-1,\delta},\phi_{h}^{n})+a_{h}(W_{h}^{n,\delta},\phi_{h}^{n})=0.

Lastly, we require an appropriate notion of a discrete inverse Laplacian,
\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}
, as defined in Appendix
A
.
With these considerations we can decouple the equations (
5.3
), (
5.4
).
To do so we notice that

\frac{1}{\tau}a_{h}(\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}(U_{h}^{n,\delta}-U_{h,+}^{n-1,\delta}),\phi_{h}^{n})+a_{h}(W_{h}^{n,\delta},\phi_{h}^{n})=0,

and hence one finds

(5.16)

\displaystyle W_{h}^{n,\delta}=\lambda_{h}^{n,\delta}-\frac{1}{\tau}\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}(U_{h}^{n,\delta}-U_{h,+}^{n-1,\delta}),

where (
5.4
) implies

\lambda_{h}^{n,\delta}=\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{h}^{n}}\left(\frac{\theta}{2\varepsilon}I_{h}f^{\delta}(U_{h}^{n,\delta})-\frac{1}{\varepsilon}U_{h}^{n,\delta}\right).

Hence the system (
5.3
), (
5.4
) can be written as a single equation,

(5.17)

\varepsilon a_{h}\left(U_{h}^{n,\delta},\phi_{h}^{n}\right)+\frac{\theta}{2\varepsilon}\bar{m}_{h}\left(f^{\delta}\left(U_{h}^{n,\delta}\right),\phi_{h}^{n}-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{h}^{n}}\phi_{h}^{n}\right)-\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\bar{m}_{h}\left(U_{h}^{n,\delta},\phi_{h}^{n}-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{h}^{n}}\phi_{h}^{n}\right)\\
+\frac{1}{\tau}\bar{m}_{h}\left(\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}(U_{h}^{n,\delta}-U^{n-1,\delta}_{h,+}),\phi_{h}^{n}\right)=0.

This observation motivates one to define a functional,
J_{h}^{n,\delta}:D^{n}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
given by

J_{h}^{n,\delta}(z_{h}^{n}):=\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\bar{m}_{h}\left(F^{\delta}(z_{h}^{n}),1\right)+\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}z_{h}^{n}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}^{2}+\frac{1}{2\tau}\|z_{h}^{n}-U_{h,+}^{n-1,\delta}\|_{-h}^{2},

where

D^{n}:=\left\{z_{h}^{n}\in S_{h}^{n}\mid\bar{m}_{h}(z_{h}^{n},1)=\bar{m}_{h}(U_{h}^{n-1,\delta},1)\right\}.

D^{n}
is clearly an affine subspace of
S_{h}^{n}
, and thus finite dimensional.

Lemma 5.7
.

Let
U_{h,0}\in\mathcal{I}_{h,0}
.
Then there exists a solution pair
(U_{h}^{n,\delta},W_{h}^{n,\delta})
solving (
5.3
), (
5.4
).

Proof.

With the observation that
U_{h}^{n,\delta}
solves (
5.17
), one may argue by the same logic as in
[
35
, Lemma 3.5]
— as such we omit further details.
Moreover, by arguing as in
[
35
, Lemma 3.1]
one finds that this solution is unique under the assumption that
\varepsilon<4\varepsilon^{3}
.

∎

Now as in Section
4
we need to establish bounds independent of
\delta
to consider the limit
\delta\rightarrow 0
.
As before, this will make use of our assumption that
\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}\cdot V_{h}\geq 0
.

Lemma 5.8
.

Let
\tau>0
be sufficiently small and such that
\tau\leq\frac{\varepsilon^{3}}{2}
.
Then the unique solution
(U_{h}^{n,\delta},W_{h}^{n,\delta})
of (
5.3
), (
5.4
) satisfies for
1\leq N\leq\lfloor\frac{T}{\tau}\rfloor
,

(5.18)

\displaystyle E^{\text{CH},\delta}_{h}[U_{h}^{N,\delta}]+\frac{\varepsilon\tau^{2}}{4}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}\partial^{\bullet}_{\tau}U_{h}^{n,\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}^{2}+\tau\sum_{n=1}^{N}\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}W_{h}^{n,\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}^{2}\leq C\exp\left(Ct_{N}\right),

where
C
denotes a constant independent of
\delta,\tau,h
.

Proof.

We begin by writing (
5.3
), (
5.4
) in matrix form as

(5.19)

\displaystyle\bar{M}^{n}\frac{(U^{n,\delta}-U^{n-1,\delta})}{\tau}+\frac{(\bar{M}^{n}-\bar{M}^{n-1})}{\tau}U^{n-1,\delta}+A^{n}W^{n,\delta}=0,

(5.20)

\displaystyle\bar{M}^{n}W^{n,\delta}=\varepsilon A^{n}U^{n,\delta}+\frac{\theta}{2\varepsilon}\bar{M}^{n}f^{\delta}(U^{n,\delta})-\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\bar{M}^{n}U^{n,\delta},

where
U^{n,\delta},W^{n,\delta}
denote the vector of nodal values of
U_{h}^{n,\delta},W_{h}^{n,\delta}
respectively, and

\displaystyle(\bar{M}^{n})_{ij}

\displaystyle=\bar{m}_{h}(t_{n};\phi_{i}^{n},\phi_{j}^{n}),

\displaystyle(A^{n})_{ij}

\displaystyle=a_{h}(t_{n};\phi_{i}^{n},\phi_{j}^{n}),

for
\phi_{i}^{n}
the ‘
i
’th nodal basis function in
S_{h}^{n}
.
Here we understand
f^{\delta}(U^{n,\delta})
as the vector with entries
f^{\delta}(U^{n,\delta}_{i})
, as we justified in Section
4
.
We dot (
5.19
) with
W^{n,\delta}
and (
5.20
) with
\frac{1}{\tau}(U^{n,\delta}-U^{n-1,\delta})
.
This yields

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}\frac{\varepsilon}{\tau}U^{n,\delta}\cdot A^{n}(U^{n,\delta}-U^{n-1,\delta})+\frac{\theta}{2\tau\varepsilon}(U^{n,\delta}-U^{n-1,\delta})\cdot\bar{M}^{n}f^{\delta}(U^{n,\delta})+W^{n,\delta}\cdot A^{n}W^{n,\delta}\\
+W^{n,\delta}\cdot\frac{(\bar{M}^{n}-\bar{M}^{n-1})}{\tau}U^{n-1,\delta}=\frac{1}{\tau\varepsilon}U^{n,\delta}\cdot\bar{M}^{n}(U^{n,\delta}-U^{n-1,\delta}).\end{gathered}

It is then straightforward to verify that

(5.23)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \frac{\varepsilon}{\tau}U^{n,\delta}\cdot A^{n}(U^{n,\delta}-U^{n-1,\delta})&=\frac{\varepsilon}{2\tau}\left[U^{n,\delta}\cdot A^{n}U^{n,\delta}-U^{n-1,\delta}\cdot A^{n-1}U^{n-1,\delta}\right]\\
&+\frac{\varepsilon}{2\tau}\left(U^{n,\delta}-U^{n-1,\delta}\right)\cdot A^{n}\left(U^{n,\delta}-U^{n-1,\delta}\right)\\
&+\frac{\varepsilon}{2\tau}\left[U^{n-1,\delta}\cdot A^{n-1}U^{n-1,\delta}-U^{n-1,\delta}\cdot A^{n}U^{n-1,\delta}\right],\end{aligned}

which we can further write as

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \frac{\varepsilon}{\tau}U^{n,\delta}\cdot A^{n}(U^{n,\delta}-U^{n-1,\delta})&=\frac{\varepsilon}{2\tau}\left[\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}U_{h}^{n,\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}^{2}-\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}U_{h}^{n-1,\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n-1})}^{2}\right]\\
&+\frac{\varepsilon\tau}{2}\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}\partial^{\bullet}_{\tau}U_{h}^{n,\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}^{2}\\
&+\frac{\varepsilon}{2\tau}\left[a_{h}(U_{h}^{n-1,\delta},U_{h}^{n-1,\delta})-a_{h}(\overline{U_{h}^{n-1,\delta}},\overline{U_{h}^{n-1,\delta}})\right].\end{aligned}

This can be understood as a fully discrete form of the transport theorem, see
[
25
, Lemma 3.5]
.

Next we turn to the term
W^{n,\delta}\cdot\frac{(\bar{M}^{n}-\bar{M}^{n-1})}{\tau}U^{n-1,\delta}
, which we deal with in an analogous way to Lemma
4.3
.
Firstly we recall that, due to mass-lumping,
\bar{M}^{n},\bar{M}^{n-1}
are diagonal matrices, and hence if we define
5
5

5

Note that
\bar{G}^{n}\neq\bar{G}(t_{n})
for
\bar{G}(t)
as defined in Section
4
, but
\bar{G}^{n}
is a finite difference approximation of
\bar{G}(t_{n})
.

\bar{G}^{n}:=\frac{(\bar{M}^{n}-\bar{M}^{n-1})}{\tau},

then
\bar{G}^{n}
is a diagonal matrix, and we show that since
\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}\cdot V_{h}\geq 0
it is also positive semi-definite.
This follows since

\displaystyle\bar{G}^{n}_{ii}=\frac{(\bar{M}_{ii}^{n}-\bar{M}_{ii}^{n-1})}{\tau}=\frac{1}{\tau}\left(\int_{\Gamma_{h}^{n}}\phi_{i}^{n}-\int_{\Gamma_{h}^{n-1}}\phi_{i}^{n-1}\right)=\frac{1}{\tau}\int_{t_{n-1}}^{t_{n}}\frac{d}{dt}m_{h}(\phi_{i}(t),1)=\underbrace{\frac{1}{\tau}\int_{t_{n-1}}^{t_{n}}g_{h}(\phi_{i}(t),1)}_{\geq 0},

since
\phi_{i}(t)\geq 0
and we have assumed
\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}\cdot V_{h}\geq 0
.
Now as
\bar{M}^{n}
is invertible one finds that

(5.24)

\displaystyle W^{n,\delta}\cdot\bar{G}^{n}U^{n-1,\delta}=\left(\varepsilon(\bar{M}^{n})^{-1}A^{n}U^{n,\delta}+\frac{\theta}{2\varepsilon}f(U^{n,\delta})-\frac{1}{\varepsilon}U^{n,\delta}\right)\cdot\bar{G}^{n}U^{n-1,\delta}.

Since
\bar{G}^{n}
is symmetric this first term may be rewritten as

(\bar{M}^{n})^{-1}A^{n}U^{n,\delta}\cdot\bar{G}^{n}U^{n-1,\delta}=U^{n-1,\delta}\cdot\bar{G}^{n}(\bar{M}^{n})^{-1}A^{n}U^{n,\delta},

and we shall defer treatment of this term until the end of the proof.
For now we shall only deal with the the potential term, for which we observe that the left-hand side of (
5.2.2
) contains

\frac{\theta}{2\tau\varepsilon}f^{\delta}(U^{n,\delta})\cdot\bar{M}^{n}(U^{n,\delta}-U^{n-1,\delta})+\frac{\theta}{2\varepsilon}f^{\delta}(U^{n,\delta})\cdot\bar{G}^{n}U^{n-1,\delta}\\
=\frac{\theta}{2\varepsilon}f^{\delta}(U^{n,\delta})\cdot\bar{G}^{n}U^{n,\delta}+\frac{\theta}{2\tau\varepsilon}f^{\delta}(U^{n,\delta})\cdot\bar{M}^{n}(U^{n,\delta}-U^{n-1,\delta})-\frac{\theta}{2\varepsilon}f^{\delta}(U^{n,\delta})\cdot\bar{G}^{n}(U^{n,\delta}-U^{n-1,\delta}).

For this first term we notice that as
\bar{G}^{n}
is diagonal and positive semi-definite, and
rf^{\delta}(r)\geq 0
one has

f^{\delta}(U^{n,\delta})\cdot\bar{G}^{n}U^{n,\delta}=\sum_{i=1}^{N_{h}}f^{\delta}(U_{i}^{n,\delta})U_{i}^{n,\delta}\bar{G}^{n}_{ii}\geq 0

Next use the definition of
\bar{G}^{n}
to see that

\frac{\theta}{2\tau\varepsilon}f^{\delta}(U^{n,\delta})\cdot\bar{M}^{n}(U^{n,\delta}-U^{n-1,\delta})-\frac{\theta}{2\varepsilon}f^{\delta}(U^{n,\delta})\cdot\bar{G}^{n}(U^{n,\delta}-U^{n-1,\delta})\\
=\frac{\theta}{2\tau\varepsilon}f^{\delta}(U^{n,\delta})\cdot\bar{M}^{n-1}(U^{n,\delta}-U^{n-1,\delta}).

In terms of our bilinear forms this is

\frac{\theta}{2\tau\varepsilon}f^{\delta}(U^{n,\delta})\cdot\bar{M}^{n-1}(U^{n}-U^{n-1})=\frac{\theta}{2\tau\varepsilon}\bar{m}_{h}(\underline{I_{h}f^{\delta}(U_{h}^{n,\delta})},\underline{U_{h}^{n,\delta}}-U_{h}^{n-1,\delta}),

and now using the convexity of
F_{\log}^{\delta}(\cdot)
one finds that

\frac{\theta}{2\tau\varepsilon}\bar{m}_{h}(\underline{I_{h}f^{\delta}(U_{h}^{n,\delta})},\underline{U_{h}^{n,\delta}}-U_{h}^{n-1,\delta})\geq\frac{\theta}{2\tau\varepsilon}\bar{m}_{h}(\underline{I_{h}F_{\log}^{\delta}(U_{h}^{n,\delta})}-I_{h}F_{\log}^{\delta}(U_{h}^{n-1,\delta}),1).

This is essentially the correct term for the
convex part
of the potential, but it remains to add the
quadratic part
, i.e. terms to do with the
\frac{1-r^{2}}{2}
term in
F^{\delta}(r)
.
To retrieve the quadratic part of the potential we observe that the right-hand side of (
5.2.2
) can be expressed as

\frac{1}{\tau\varepsilon}U^{n,\delta}\cdot\bar{M}^{n}(U^{n,\delta}-U^{n-1,\delta})=\frac{1}{2\tau\varepsilon}\left(U^{n,\delta}\cdot\bar{M}^{n}U^{n,\delta}-U^{n-1,\delta}\cdot\bar{M}^{n-1}U^{n-1,\delta}\right)\\
+\frac{1}{2\tau\varepsilon}(U^{n,\delta}-U^{n-1,\delta})\cdot\bar{M}^{n}(U^{n,\delta}-U^{n-1,\delta})+\frac{1}{2\tau\varepsilon}\left[U^{n-1,\delta}\cdot\bar{M}^{n-1}U^{n-1,\delta}-U^{n-1,\delta}\cdot\bar{M}^{n}U^{n-1,\delta}\right],

where this first term is essentially the correct term for the quadratic part of the potential.
From this we have that

(5.25)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \frac{1}{\tau\varepsilon}U^{n,\delta}\cdot\bar{M}^{n}(U^{n,\delta}-U^{n-1,\delta})&=-\frac{1}{\tau\varepsilon}\left[\bar{m}_{h}\left(\frac{1-(U_{h}^{n,\delta})^{2}}{2},1\right)-\bar{m}_{h}\left(\frac{1-(U_{h}^{n-1,\delta})^{2}}{2},1\right)\right]\\
&+\frac{1}{2\tau\varepsilon}\left[\bar{m}_{h}(t_{n};1,1)-\bar{m}_{h}(t_{n-1};1,1)\right]\\
&+\frac{\tau}{2\varepsilon}\|\partial^{\bullet}_{\tau}U_{h}^{n,\delta}\|_{h,t_{n}}^{2}\\
&+\frac{1}{2\tau\varepsilon}\left[\bar{m}_{h}(U_{h}^{n-1,\delta},U_{h}^{n-1,\delta})-\bar{m}_{h}(\overline{U_{h}^{n-1,\delta}},\overline{U_{h}^{n-1,\delta}})\right],\end{aligned}

where we have introduced extra terms to match the form of the quadratic part of the potential.
It is straightforward to see that

\frac{1}{2\tau\varepsilon}\left[\bar{m}_{h}(t_{n};1,1)-\bar{m}_{h}(t_{n-1};1,1)\right]=\frac{(|\Gamma_{h}^{n}|-|\Gamma_{h}^{n-1}|)}{2\tau\varepsilon}.

Combining (
5.23
), (
5.25
) in (
5.2.2
) one finds

(5.26)

\frac{\varepsilon}{2\tau}\left(\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}U_{h}^{n,\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}^{2}-\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}U_{h}^{n-1,\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n-1})}^{2}\right)+\frac{1}{\tau\varepsilon}\left(\int_{\Gamma_{h}^{n}}I_{h}F^{\delta}(U_{h}^{n,\delta})-\int_{\Gamma_{h}^{n-1}}I_{h}F^{\delta}(U_{h}^{n-1,\delta})\right)\\
+\frac{\varepsilon\tau}{2}\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}\partial^{\bullet}_{\tau}U_{h}^{n,\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}^{2}+\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}W_{h}^{n,\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}^{2}\leq C+\sum_{k=1}^{6}I_{k},

where

\displaystyle I_{1}:=\frac{\varepsilon}{2\tau}\left[a_{h}(\overline{U_{h}^{n-1,\delta}},\overline{U_{h}^{n-1,\delta}})-a_{h}(U_{h}^{n-1,\delta},U_{h}^{n-1,\delta})\right],

\displaystyle I_{2}:=\frac{\theta}{2\tau\varepsilon}\left[\bar{m}_{h}({I_{h}F_{\log}^{\delta}(U_{h}^{n,\delta})},1)-\bar{m}_{h}(\underline{I_{h}F_{\log}^{\delta}(U_{h}^{n,\delta})},1)\right],

\displaystyle I_{3}:=\frac{1}{\varepsilon}U^{n,\delta}\cdot\bar{G}^{n}U^{n-1,\delta}=\frac{1}{\tau\varepsilon}\left[\bar{m}_{h}(U_{h}^{n,\delta},\overline{U_{h}^{n-1,\delta}})-\bar{m}_{h}(\underline{U_{h}^{n,\delta}},U_{h}^{n-1,\delta})\right],

\displaystyle I_{4}:=\frac{1}{2\tau\varepsilon}\left[\bar{m}_{h}(\overline{U_{h}^{n-1,\delta}},\overline{U_{h}^{n-1,\delta}})-\bar{m}_{h}(U_{h}^{n-1,\delta},U_{h}^{n-1,\delta})\right],

\displaystyle I_{5}:=\frac{\tau}{2\varepsilon}\|\partial^{\bullet}_{\tau}U_{h}^{n,\delta}\|_{h,t_{n}}^{2},

\displaystyle I_{6}:=-\varepsilon U^{n-1,\delta}\cdot\bar{G}^{n}(\bar{M}^{n})^{-1}A^{n}U^{n,\delta},

and
C
is a constant, independent of
\delta,h,\tau
, such that

\left|\frac{|\Gamma_{h}^{n}|-|\Gamma_{h}^{n-1}|}{2\tau\varepsilon}\right|\leq C,

which one can obtain from the smoothness of
V
.
It is important to note that now the potential term in (
5.26
) is in terms of the full potential,
F^{\delta}
, not just the convex part,
F^{\delta}_{\log}
.

We now bound each of these terms.
Firstly, we find that

(5.27)

\displaystyle|I_{1}|\leq C\varepsilon\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}U_{h}^{n-1,\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n-1})}^{2},

from (
5.8
).
Similarly we observe that one obtains

(5.28)

\displaystyle|I_{2}|\leq\frac{C}{\varepsilon}\bar{m}_{h}(I_{h}F_{\log}^{\delta}(U_{h}^{n,\delta}),1),

(5.29)

\displaystyle|I_{3}|\leq\frac{C}{\varepsilon}\|U_{h}^{n,\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}\|U_{h}^{n-1,\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n-1})},

(5.30)

\displaystyle|I_{4}|\leq\frac{C}{\varepsilon}\|U_{h}^{n-1,\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n-1})}^{2},

from using (
5.9
) (and (
2.24
) where necessary).

It remains to bound
I_{5}
and
I_{6}
, which are the two most problematic terms.
Firstly, to bound
I_{5}
we notice that we may write (
5.3
) as

\bar{m}_{h}(\partial^{\bullet}_{\tau}U_{h}^{n,\delta},\phi_{h}^{n})+\frac{1}{\tau}\left(\bar{m}_{h}(\overline{U_{h}^{n-1,\delta}},\phi_{h}^{n})-\bar{m}_{h}({U_{h}^{n-1,\delta}},\underline{\phi_{h}^{n}})\right)+a_{h}(W_{h}^{n,\delta},\phi_{h}^{n})=0.

We test this with
\phi_{h}^{n}=\frac{\tau}{2\varepsilon}\partial^{\bullet}_{\tau}U_{h}^{n,\delta}
to see that

I_{5}=-\frac{1}{2\varepsilon}\left(\bar{m}_{h}(\overline{U_{h}^{n-1,\delta}},\partial^{\bullet}_{\tau}U_{h}^{n,\delta})-\bar{m}_{h}({U_{h}^{n-1,\delta}},\underline{\partial^{\bullet}_{\tau}U_{h}^{n,\delta}})\right)-\frac{\tau}{2\varepsilon}a_{h}(W_{h}^{n,\delta},\partial^{\bullet}_{\tau}U_{h}^{n,\delta}).

Recalling that
I_{5}=\frac{\tau}{2\varepsilon}\|\partial^{\bullet}_{\tau}U_{h}^{n,\delta}\|_{h,t_{n}}^{2}
, we may use (
5.9
) and Young’s inequality to find that

(5.31)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} |I_{5}|&\leq C\tau\|U_{h}^{n-1,\delta}\|_{h,t_{n-1}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}W_{h}^{n,\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}^{2}+\frac{\tau^{2}}{2\varepsilon^{2}}\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}\partial^{\bullet}_{\tau}U_{h}^{n,\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}^{2}\\
&\leq C\tau\|U_{h}^{n-1,\delta}\|_{h,t_{n-1}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}W_{h}^{n,\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}^{2}+\frac{\varepsilon\tau}{4}\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}\partial^{\bullet}_{\tau}U_{h}^{n,\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}^{2},\end{aligned}

where we have used the assumption
\tau\leq\frac{\varepsilon^{3}}{2}
.
We now turn to
I_{6}
, which is dealt with by similar means to the proof of Lemma
4.3
.
We define the function
\widetilde{U_{h}^{n,\delta}}\in S_{h}^{n}
to be the unique solution of

\bar{m}_{h}(\widetilde{U_{h}^{n,\delta}},\phi_{h}^{n})=a_{h}({U_{h}^{n,\delta}},\phi_{h}^{n}),

for all
\phi_{h}^{n}\in S_{h}^{n}
.
As in (
4.8
) one finds that

\|\widetilde{U_{h}^{n,\delta}}\|_{h,t_{n}}\leq\frac{C}{{h}}\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}U_{h}^{n,\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})},

for a constant independent of
\tau,h
.
Denoting the vector of nodal values of
\widetilde{U_{h}^{n,\delta}}
as
\widetilde{U^{n,\delta}}
then one finds that
\widetilde{U^{n,\delta}}=(\bar{M}^{n})^{-1}A^{n}{U^{n,\delta}}
.
Hence we find that

|I_{6}|=\varepsilon|U^{n-1,\delta}\cdot\bar{G}^{n}\widetilde{U^{n,\delta}}|=\frac{\varepsilon}{\tau}|\bar{m}_{h}(\overline{U_{h}^{n-1,\delta}},\widetilde{U_{h}^{n,\delta}})-\bar{m}_{h}({U_{h}^{n-1,\delta}},\underline{\widetilde{U_{h}^{n,\delta}}})|.

The idea is now to write

\displaystyle\bar{m}_{h}(\overline{U_{h}^{n-1,\delta}},\widetilde{U_{h}^{n,\delta}})-\bar{m}_{h}({U_{h}^{n-1,\delta}},\underline{\widetilde{U_{h}^{n,\delta}}})

\displaystyle=\int_{t_{n-1}}^{t_{n}}\frac{d}{dt}\bar{m}_{h}(\overline{U_{h}^{n-1,\delta}}(s),\underline{\widetilde{U_{h}^{n,\delta}}}(s))\,ds

\displaystyle=\int_{t_{n-1}}^{t_{n}}g_{h}(I_{h}(\overline{U_{h}^{n-1,\delta}}(s)\underline{\widetilde{U_{h}^{n,\delta}}}(s)),1)\,ds.

Now one proceeds almost identically to Lemma
4.3
(as well as using (
5.10
)) to find that

|g_{h}(I_{h}(\overline{U_{h}^{n-1,\delta}}(s)\underline{\widetilde{U_{h}^{n,\delta}}}(s)),1)|\leq C+CE^{\text{CH},\delta}_{h}[U_{h}^{n-1,\delta}]+CE^{\text{CH},\delta}_{h}[U_{h}^{n,\delta}].

In the interest of brevity we do not expound upon these details.
The end result of this calculation is that

(5.32)

\displaystyle|I_{6}|\leq C+CE^{\text{CH},\delta}_{h}[U_{h}^{n-1,\delta}]+CE^{\text{CH},\delta}_{h}[U_{h}^{n,\delta}]..

Combining (
5.27
)–(
5.32
) in (
5.26
), and using Young/Poincaré inequalities accordingly, one readily obtains

E^{\text{CH},\delta}_{h}[U_{h}^{N,\delta}]-E^{\text{CH},\delta}_{h}[U_{h,0}]+\frac{\varepsilon\tau^{2}}{4}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}\partial^{\bullet}_{\tau}U_{h}^{n,\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}^{2}+\tau\sum_{n=1}^{N}\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}W_{h}^{N,\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}^{2}\\
\leq C+C\tau\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}E^{\text{CH},\delta}_{h}[U_{h}^{n,\delta}]+C\tau E^{\text{CH},\delta}_{h}[U_{h}^{N,\delta}].

The result follows from a discrete Grönwall inequality, provided
\tau
is sufficiently small.
This can then be bounded independent of
\delta,h
by similar arguments to Lemma
4.3
.
∎

We also obtain slightly stronger bounds on the derivative by considering weaker norms.
This is the content of the following lemma, which will be invaluable for our later error analysis.

Lemma 5.9
.

Under the assumptions in the previous lemma, and assuming that a Courant-Friedrichs-Lewy (CFL) condition,
\tau\leq Ch^{2}
, holds then one has that for
1\leq N\leq N_{T}
,

(5.33)

\displaystyle\tau^{\frac{3}{2}}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\|\partial^{\bullet}_{\tau}U_{h}^{n,\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}^{2}\leq C,

(5.34)

\displaystyle\tau\sum_{n=1}^{N}\left\|\partial^{\bullet}_{\tau}U_{h}^{n,\delta}-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.98003pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.26338pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.6363pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.45924pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{h}^{n}}\partial^{\bullet}_{\tau}U_{h}^{n,\delta}\right\|_{-h}^{2}\leq C,

for a constant
C
independent of
\delta,\tau,h
.

Proof.

We first prove (
5.33
), noting from (
2.24
) that

\displaystyle\tau^{\frac{3}{2}}\|\partial^{\bullet}_{\tau}U_{h}^{n,\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{\tau}}\left\|U_{h}^{n,\delta}-U_{h,+}^{n-1,\delta}\right\|_{h,t_{n}}^{2}+\frac{C}{\sqrt{\tau}}\left\|U_{h,+}^{n-1,\delta}-\overline{U_{h}^{n-1,\delta}}\right\|_{h,t_{n}}^{2}

From (
5.14
) one immediately finds

\frac{C}{\sqrt{\tau}}\left\|U_{h,+}^{n-1,\delta}-\overline{U_{h}^{n-1,\delta}}\right\|_{h,t_{n}}^{2}\leq C\tau^{\frac{3}{2}}\|U_{h}^{n-1,\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n-1})}^{2},

and hence we sum over
N=1,...,N
and use the mass-conservation property of
U_{h}^{n,\delta}
along with the Poincaré inequality for

\frac{C}{\sqrt{\tau}}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\left\|U_{h,+}^{n-1,\delta}-\overline{U_{h}^{n-1,\delta}}\right\|_{h,t_{n}}^{2}\leq C\tau^{\frac{3}{2}}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\left(1+\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}U_{h}^{n-1,\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n-1})}^{2}\right)\leq C.

Here the final inequality follows by using (
5.18
), the fact that
N\tau\leq T
and the upper bound on
\tau
.

The other term will be bounded by an interpolation argument.
Firstly we notice that

\displaystyle\left\|U_{h}^{n,\delta}-U_{h,+}^{n-1,\delta}\right\|_{h,t_{n}}^{2}

\displaystyle=a_{h}(\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}(U_{h}^{n,\delta}-U_{h,+}^{n-1,\delta}),U_{h}^{n,\delta}-U_{h,+}^{n-1,\delta})

\displaystyle\leq\left\|U_{h}^{n,\delta}-U_{h,+}^{n-1,\delta}\right\|_{-h}\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}(U_{h}^{n,\delta}-U_{h,+}^{n-1,\delta})\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}.

We now establish a bound on
\left\|\left(U_{h}^{n,\delta}-U_{h,+}^{n-1,\delta}\right)\right\|_{-h}
.
To do this we test (
5.15
) with
\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\left(U_{h}^{n,\delta}-U_{h,+}^{n-1,\delta}\right)
to obtain

\displaystyle\left\|U_{h}^{n,\delta}-U_{h,+}^{n-1,\delta}\right\|_{-h}^{2}=-\tau a_{h}\left(W_{h}^{n,\delta},\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\left(U_{h}^{n,\delta}-U_{h,+}^{n-1,\delta}\right)\right),

from which Young’s inequality yields

\left\|U_{h}^{n,\delta}-U_{h,+}^{n-1,\delta}\right\|_{-h}\leq\tau\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}W_{h}^{n,\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})},

and by using (
5.18
) it follows that

\frac{1}{\tau}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\left\|U_{h}^{n,\delta}-U_{h,+}^{n-1,\delta}\right\|_{-h}^{2}\leq\tau\sum_{n=1}^{N}\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}W_{h}^{n,\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}^{2}\leq C.

By using Hölder’s inequality we find that

\frac{1}{\sqrt{\tau}}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\left\|U_{h}^{n,\delta}-U_{h,+}^{n-1,\delta}\right\|_{h,t_{n}}^{2}\leq\left(\frac{1}{\tau}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\left\|U_{h}^{n,\delta}-U_{h,+}^{n-1,\delta}\right\|_{-h}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\sum_{n=1}^{N}\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}(U_{h}^{n,\delta}-U_{h,+}^{n-1,\delta})\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},

where we claim that the rightmost term is bounded independent of
\delta,\tau,h
.
To see this is true we write

\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{N}\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}(U_{h}^{n,\delta}-U_{h,+}^{n-1,\delta})\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\tau^{2}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}\partial^{\bullet}_{\tau}U_{h}^{n,\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}^{2}+2\sum_{n=1}^{N}\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}(\overline{U_{h}^{n-1,\delta}}-U_{h,+}^{n-1,\delta})\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C+\frac{C\tau^{2}}{h^{2}}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\|U_{h}^{n-1,\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n-1})}^{2}\leq C,

where we have used (
5.14
), (
5.18
), an inverse inequality, our CFL condition
\tau\leq Ch^{2}
, the bound
N\tau\leq T
, as well as using the Poincaré inequality in the usual way.
Combining all of this together appropriately yields (
5.33
).

To show (
5.34
) we observe that (
5.3
) can be written as

\bar{m}_{h}(\partial^{\bullet}_{\tau}U_{h}^{n,\delta},\phi_{h}^{n})+\frac{1}{\tau}\left(\bar{m}_{h}(\overline{U_{h}^{n-1,\delta}},\phi_{h}^{n})-\bar{m}_{h}(U_{h}^{n-1,\delta},\underline{\phi_{h}^{n}})\right)+a_{h}(W_{h}^{n,\delta},\phi_{h}^{n})=0.

We test the above with
\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}(\partial^{\bullet}_{\tau}U_{h}^{n,\delta}-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{h}^{n}}\partial^{\bullet}_{\tau}U_{h}^{n,\delta})
to see that

\tau\left\|\partial^{\bullet}_{\tau}U_{h}^{n,\delta}-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{h}^{n}}\partial^{\bullet}_{\tau}U_{h}^{n,\delta}\right\|_{-h}^{2}=-\tau a_{h}\left(W_{h}^{n,\delta},\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\left(\partial^{\bullet}_{\tau}U_{h}^{n,\delta}-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{h}^{n}}\partial^{\bullet}_{\tau}U_{h}^{n,\delta}\right)\right)\\
+\left(\bar{m}_{h}\left(U_{h}^{n-1,\delta},\underline{\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\left(\partial^{\bullet}_{\tau}U_{h}^{n,\delta}-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{h}^{n}}\partial^{\bullet}_{\tau}U_{h}^{n,\delta}\right)}\right)-\bar{m}_{h}\left(\overline{U_{h}^{n-1,\delta}},\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\left(\partial^{\bullet}_{\tau}U_{h}^{n,\delta}-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{h}^{n}}\partial^{\bullet}_{\tau}U_{h}^{n,\delta}\right)\right)\right).

(
5.34
) now follows by using Young/Poincaré inequalities, (
5.9
), and (
5.18
), where one bounds the
\|U_{h}^{n-1,\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n-1})}
term as we have above.
∎

Next we state an analogue of Lemma
4.6
, which allows us to control the measure of the sets
\{|U_{h}^{n,\delta}|>1\}\subset\Gamma_{h}^{n}
.
This is identical to the proof of Lemma
4.6
, which considers a fixed time
t\in[0,T]
.

Lemma 5.10
.

There exist constants
C_{1},C_{2}>0
, independent of
\delta\in(0,1)
, such that for
n\geq 1

(5.35)

\int_{\Gamma_{h}^{n}}[-1-U_{h}^{n,\delta}]_{+}+\int_{\Gamma_{h}^{n}}[U_{h}^{n,\delta}-1]_{+}\leq C\left(\frac{1}{|\log(\delta)|}+\delta\right).

As in the semi-discrete case this is used in bounding the potential term, as in the following lemma which is a discrete time analogue of Lemma
4.7
.

Lemma 5.11
.

For
N\geq 1
, and sufficiently small
\delta
we have the following bounds,

(5.36)

\displaystyle\tau\sum_{n=1}^{N}\left\|I_{h}f^{\delta}(U_{h}^{n,\delta})-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.98003pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.26338pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.6363pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.45924pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{h}^{n}}I_{h}f^{\delta}(U_{h}^{n,\delta})\right\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}^{2}\leq C,

(5.37)

\displaystyle\tau\sum_{n=1}^{N}\left\|I_{h}f^{\delta}(U_{h}^{n,\delta})\right\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}^{2}\leq C,

where
C
denotes a constant independent of
\delta,\tau
.

We omit the proof of this result as it is essentially identical to Lemma
4.7
.
As in the semi-discrete case, this allows one to establish
\delta
-independent bounds on
\tau\sum_{n=1}^{N_{T}}\|W_{h}^{n,\delta}\|_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}^{2}
by using the Poincaré inequality and (
5.18
) (and obtaining an appropriate bound on
\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{h}^{n}}W_{h}^{n,\delta}
analogously to the argument in Section
4
).

5.2.3.
Passage to the limit

We now use the established uniform bounds to pass to the limit as
\delta\searrow 0
.
By the compact embedding
H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})\overset{c}{\hookrightarrow}L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})
we can find a subsequence of
\delta\searrow 0
such that
U_{h}^{n,\delta}\rightarrow U_{h}^{n}
and
W_{h}^{n,\delta}\rightarrow W_{h}^{n}
strongly in
L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})
, where
U_{h}^{n},W_{h}^{n}
are the appropriate limits in
S_{h}^{n}
.
Thus we obtain pointwise convergence for almost all
x\in\Gamma_{h}^{n}
, and by arguing as in Lemma
4.10
this is in fact convergence for all
x\in\Gamma_{h}^{n}
.

Similar to the semi-discrete solution, we have that
|U_{h}^{n}|\leq 1
almost everywhere on
\Gamma_{h}^{n}
for
n\geq 1
.
We must again strengthen this to be a strict inequality, for which one can repeat the arguments of Lemma
4.11
.
Using this, the continuity of
f
away from
\pm 1
, and the convergence
U_{h}^{n,\delta}(x_{i})\rightarrow U_{h}^{n}(x_{i})
we find

f^{\delta}(U_{h}^{n,\delta}(x_{i}))=f(U_{h}^{n,\delta}(x_{i}))\rightarrow f(U_{h}^{n}(x_{i})),

for each of the nodes
x_{i}\in\Gamma_{h}^{n}
.
Note that this implies pointwise convergence everywhere on
\Gamma_{h}^{n}
hence we also obtain strong convergence
I_{h}f^{\delta}(U_{h}^{n,\delta})\rightarrow I_{h}f(U_{h}^{n})
in
L^{p}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})
for
p\in[1,\infty]
as the maximum of these functions occurs at the nodes of
\Gamma_{h}(t)
.
Hence by passing to the limit as
\delta\rightarrow 0
in (
5.3
), (
5.4
) we obtain solutions of (
5.1
), (
5.2
).
We end this subsection by stating a discrete time analogue of Proposition
4.12
.

Proposition 5.12
.

Let
U_{h,0}^{1},U_{h,0}^{2}\in\mathcal{I}_{h,0}
be initial data with
\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.98003pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.26338pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.6363pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.45924pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{h}(0)}U_{h,0}^{1}=\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.98003pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.26338pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.6363pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.45924pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{h}(0)}U_{h,0}^{2}
.
We denote the corresponding solution pair of (
5.1
), (
5.2
) by
(U_{h}^{n,i},W_{h}^{n,i})
, for
i=1,2
.
Then if
\tau<\varepsilon^{3}
, we have for all
N\geq 1
,

\left\|U_{h}^{N,1}-U_{h}^{N,2}\right\|_{-h}^{2}+\frac{\varepsilon^{4}\tau}{\varepsilon^{3}-\tau}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}(U_{h}^{n,1}-U_{h}^{n,2})\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}^{2}\leq e^{Ct_{N}}\left(\frac{\varepsilon^{3}}{\varepsilon^{3}-\tau}\right)\left\|U^{1}_{h,0}-U^{2}_{h,0}\right\|_{-h}^{2}.

Proof.

This proof is essentially identical to
[
35
, Proposition 3.1]
and is hence omitted.
The only meaningful difference is that due to the use of mass-lumping we use
\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}
rather than
\mathcal{G}_{S_{h}}
(again see Appendix
A
).
∎

Remark 5.13
.

The smallness condition on
\tau
is quite severe since in practise
\varepsilon
is very small (to approximate a sharp interface).
One could mitigate these restrictions by using an implicit-explicit scheme as in
[
35
, Section 5]
, but we leave this as a topic for future work.

5.3.
Error analysis

In this subsection we provide a similar analysis to that of the semi-discrete scheme, and prove error bounds for the fully discrete scheme.
As in the semi-discrete case, we assume
u_{0}
is such that Lemma
4.13
holds, and consider
U_{h,0}=\Pi_{h}u_{0}
.

As in
[
7
,
21
]
we construct piecewise functions in time from the values
U_{h}^{n},W_{h}^{n}
, and compare these to the semi-discrete solutions,
U_{h},W_{h}
.
The reason for this is twofold.
Firstly, we can use the error bounds we established for the semi-discrete scheme.
Secondly, if we were to show a time discrete analogue of (
3.7
) (on the true surface
\Gamma(t)
) we would require stronger regularity on the true solution,
u
.
This is not ideal as the singular potential limits the known regularity of
u
.
For contrast, we refer the reader to the analysis of a fully discrete ESFEM scheme for the Cahn-Hilliard equation with a regular potential
[
35
]
where this approach is not required.

Given our fully discrete solutions
U_{h}^{n},W_{h}^{n}
, we define piecewise linear functions

\displaystyle U_{h}^{\tau}(t):=\left(\frac{t-t_{n-1}}{\tau}\right)\Phi_{t}^{h}\Phi_{-t_{n}}^{h}U_{h}^{n}+\left(\frac{t_{n}-t}{\tau}\right)\Phi_{t}^{h}\Phi_{-t_{n-1}}^{h}U_{h}^{n-1},

\displaystyle W_{h}^{\tau}(t):=\left(\frac{t-t_{n-1}}{\tau}\right)\Phi_{t}^{h}\Phi_{-t_{n}}^{h}W_{h}^{n}+\left(\frac{t_{n}-t}{\tau}\right)\Phi_{t}^{h}\Phi_{-t_{n-1}}^{h}W_{h}^{n-1},

for
t\in[t_{n-1},t_{n}]
.
It is straightforward to see from the transport property that

\partial^{\bullet}_{h}U_{h}^{\tau}=\Phi_{t}^{h}\Phi_{-t_{n}}^{h}\partial^{\bullet}_{\tau}U_{h}^{n}=\underline{\partial^{\bullet}_{\tau}U_{h}^{n}}(t).

Similarly we define piecewise constant functions

\displaystyle\underline{U_{h}}(t):=\Phi_{t}^{h}\Phi_{-t_{n}}^{h}U_{h}^{n}=\underline{U_{h}^{n}}(t),

\displaystyle\underline{W_{h}}(t):=\Phi_{t}^{h}\Phi_{-t_{n}}^{h}W_{h}^{n}=\underline{W_{h}^{n}}(t),

for
t\in(t_{n-1},t_{n})
.
One can define
U_{h}^{\tau,\delta},W_{h}^{\tau,\delta},\underline{U_{h}^{\delta}},\underline{W_{h}^{\delta}}
similarly.
The fully discrete error bound will be established by considering the decomposition

u^{-\ell}-\underline{U_{h}}=\underbrace{[u^{-\ell}-(u^{\delta})^{-\ell}]}_{\mathcal{O}(\delta)}+\underbrace{[(u^{\delta})^{-\ell}-U_{h}^{\delta}]}_{\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{h^{4}\log\left(\frac{1}{h}\right)}{\delta^{2}}\right)}+\underbrace{[U_{h}^{\delta}-\underline{U_{h}^{\delta}}]}_{\mathcal{O}(?)}-\underbrace{[\underline{U_{h}^{\delta}}-\underline{U_{h}}]}_{\mathcal{O}(?)},

where we have bounded all but the last two terms.

We now observe that these piecewise functions solve a perturbed version of the semi-discrete system (
4.2
), (
4.3
).
Specifically we have that, since
U_{h}^{n},W_{h}^{n}
solve (
5.1
), (
5.2
) for all
\phi_{h}^{n}\in S_{h}^{n}
, and almost all
t\in[0,T]
, one has

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}\bar{m}_{h}(\partial^{\bullet}_{h}U_{h}^{\tau},\phi_{h})+g_{h}(I_{h}(\underline{U_{h}}\phi_{h}),1)+a_{h}(\underline{W_{h}},\phi_{h})=\sum_{i=1}^{3}D_{i},\\
\bar{m}_{h}(\underline{W_{h}},\phi_{h}(t))=\varepsilon a_{h}(\underline{U_{h}},\phi_{h}(t))+\frac{\theta}{2\varepsilon}\bar{m}_{h}(I_{h}f(\underline{U_{h}}),\phi_{h}(t))-\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\bar{m}_{h}(\underline{U_{h}},\phi_{h}(t))+\sum_{i=4}^{7}D_{i},\end{gathered}

for some defects
D_{i}=D_{i}(t;\phi_{h})
.
For
t\in(t_{n-1},t_{n}]
, these defects are given by

\displaystyle D_{1}(t;\phi_{h})=\bar{m}_{h}(\partial^{\bullet}_{h}U_{h}^{\tau},\phi_{h}(t))-\bar{m}_{h}(\partial^{\bullet}_{\tau}U_{h}^{n},\phi_{h}^{n}),

\displaystyle D_{2}(t;\phi_{h})=g_{h}(I_{h}(\underline{U_{h}}\phi_{h}(t)),1)-\frac{1}{\tau}\left(\bar{m}_{h}(\overline{U_{h}^{n-1}},\phi_{h}^{n})-\bar{m}_{h}(U_{h}^{n-1},\underline{\phi_{h}^{n}})\right),

\displaystyle D_{3}(t;\phi_{h})=a_{h}(\underline{W_{h}},\phi_{h}(t))-a_{h}(W_{h}^{n},\phi_{h}^{n}),

\displaystyle D_{4}(t;\phi_{h})=\bar{m}_{h}(\underline{W_{h}},\phi_{h}(t))-\bar{m}_{h}(W_{h}^{n},\phi_{h}^{n}),

\displaystyle D_{5}(t;\phi_{h})=\varepsilon\left[a_{h}(U_{h}^{n},\phi_{h}^{n})-a_{h}(\underline{U_{h}},\phi_{h}^{n}(t))\right],

\displaystyle D_{6}(t;\phi_{h})=\frac{\theta}{2\varepsilon}\left[\bar{m}_{h}(I_{h}f(U_{h}^{n}),\phi_{h}^{n})-\bar{m}_{h}(I_{h}f(\underline{U_{h}}),\phi_{h}(t))\right],

\displaystyle D_{7}(t;\phi_{h})=\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\left[\bar{m}_{h}(U_{h}^{n},\phi_{h}^{n})-\bar{m}_{h}(\underline{U_{h}},\phi_{h}(t))\right].

Here
\phi_{h}(t)\in S_{h}(t)
, and
\phi_{h}^{n}\in S_{h}^{n}
is chosen as
\phi_{h}^{n}=\Phi_{t_{n}}^{h}\Phi_{-t}^{h}\phi_{h}(t)
.
We bound these defects in the following lemma.

Lemma 5.14
.

D_{1},...,D_{7}
as defined above are bounded as

\displaystyle|D_{1}(t;\phi_{h})|\leq C\tau\|\partial^{\bullet}_{\tau}U_{h}^{n}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}\|\phi_{h}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))},

\displaystyle|D_{2}(t;\phi_{h})|\leq C\tau\left(\|U_{h}^{n}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}+\|\partial^{\bullet}_{\tau}U_{h}^{n}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}\right)\|\phi_{h}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))},

\displaystyle|D_{3}(t;\phi_{h})|\leq C\tau\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}W_{h}^{n}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}\phi_{h}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))},

\displaystyle|D_{4}(t;\phi_{h})|\leq C\tau\|W_{h}^{n}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}\|\phi_{h}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))},

\displaystyle|D_{5}(t;\phi_{h})|\leq C\tau\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}U_{h}^{n}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}\phi_{h}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))},

\displaystyle|D_{6}(t;\phi_{h})|\leq C\tau\|I_{h}f(U_{h}^{n})\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}\|\phi_{h}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))},

\displaystyle|D_{7}(t;\phi_{h})|\leq C\tau\|U_{h}^{n}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}\|\phi_{h}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}

where
C
denotes a constant independent of
t,h,\tau
.

Proof.

Most of these bounds are readily shown from (
5.7
), (
5.9
) and (
2.24
) where necessary, and we note that for
\tau
sufficiently small one has

\|\phi_{h}^{n}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}\leq C\|\phi_{h}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))},\qquad\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}\phi_{h}^{n}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}\leq C\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}\phi_{h}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}

by similar arguments to (
5.10
), (
5.11
).

The only bound which isn’t immediate is that of
D_{2}
.
To bound
D_{2}
we firstly notice that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\tau}\left(\bar{m}_{h}(\overline{U_{h}^{n-1}},\phi_{h}^{n})-\bar{m}_{h}(U_{h}^{n-1},\underline{\phi_{h}^{n}})\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\tau}\int_{t_{n-1}}^{t_{n}}\frac{d}{dt}\bar{m}_{h}(\overline{U_{h}^{n-1}}(s),\underline{\phi_{h}^{n}}(s))\,ds

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\tau}\int_{t_{n-1}}^{t_{n}}g_{h}(I_{h}(\overline{U_{h}^{n-1}}(s)\underline{\phi_{h}^{n}}(s)),1)\,ds

from which we see

D_{2}(t;\phi_{h})=\frac{1}{\tau}\int_{t_{n-1}}^{t_{n}}g_{h}(I_{h}(\underline{U_{h}}\underline{\phi_{h}^{n}}(t)),1)-g_{h}(I_{h}(\overline{U_{h}^{n-1}}(s)\underline{\phi_{h}^{n}}(s)),1)\,ds.

We then write this as
