D_{2}(t;\phi_{h})=\frac{1}{\tau}\int_{t_{n-1}}^{t_{n}}g_{h}(I_{h}(\underline{U_{h}}(t)\underline{\phi_{h}^{n}}(t)),1)-g_{h}(I_{h}(\underline{U_{h}}(s)\underline{\phi_{h}^{n}}(s)),1)\,ds\\
+\frac{1}{\tau}\int_{t_{n-1}}^{t_{n}}g_{h}(I_{h}(\underline{U_{h}}(s)\underline{\phi_{h}^{n}}(s))-I_{h}(\overline{U_{h}^{n-1}}(s)\underline{\phi_{h}^{n}}(s)),1)\,ds,

which one readily sees is

(5.40)

\displaystyle D_{2}(t;\phi_{h})=\frac{1}{\tau}\int_{t_{n-1}}^{t_{n}}\int_{s}^{t}\frac{d}{dr}g_{h}(I_{h}(\underline{U_{h}}(r)\underline{\phi_{h}^{n}}(r)),1)\,dr\,ds+\int_{t_{n-1}}^{t_{n}}g_{h}(I_{h}(\underline{\partial^{\bullet}_{\tau}U_{h}^{n}}(s)\underline{\phi_{h}^{n}}(s)),1)\,ds.

Noting that
\partial^{\bullet}_{h}I_{h}(\underline{U_{h}}\underline{\phi_{h}^{n}})=0
one can readily show that

\frac{d}{dr}g_{h}(I_{h}(\underline{U_{h}}(r)\underline{\phi_{h}^{n}}(r)),1)=\int_{\Gamma_{h}(r)}I_{h}(\underline{U_{h}}(r)\underline{\phi_{h}^{n}}(r))\left(\partial^{\bullet}_{h}(\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}\cdot V_{h})+(\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}\cdot V_{h})^{2}\right).

We do not show this but note that it follows from similar logic to
[
26
, Lemma 5.6]
.
Then as
V_{h}=I_{h}V^{-\ell}
, our assumed smoothness on
V,\partial^{\bullet}V
lets us conclude that

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\sup_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}\left|\partial^{\bullet}_{h}(\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}\cdot V_{h})+(\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}\cdot V_{h})^{2}\right|\leq C,

for some constant
C
independent of
h
.
Thus using this uniform bound in (
5.40
), and using (
5.5
) where appropriate, one readily concludes the claimed bound for
|D_{2}(t;\phi_{h})|
.
∎

One can show that
U_{h}^{\tau,\delta},\underline{U_{h}^{\delta}},\underline{W_{h}^{\delta}}
solve a similar system with defects
D_{i}^{\delta}
,
i=1,...7
.
Moreover, these defects obey almost identical bounds to those in the preceding lemma.

Next we show that
U_{h}^{\tau}
and
\underline{U_{h}}
remain close, in appropriate norms, as is the content of the following lemma.

Lemma 5.15
.

(5.41)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\|(U_{h}^{\tau}-\underline{U_{h}})\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}^{2}+\sqrt{\tau}\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}(U_{h}^{\tau}-\underline{U_{h}})\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}^{2}\leq C\tau^{\frac{3}{2}},

(5.42)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\left\|(U_{h}^{\tau}-\underline{U_{h}})-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.98003pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.26338pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.6363pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.45924pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}(U_{h}^{\tau}-\underline{U_{h}})\right\|_{-h}^{2}\leq C\tau^{2},

for some constant
C
independent of
\tau,h
.

Proof.

We start by showing the
L^{2}
bound of (
5.41
).
By definition one has

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\|U_{h}^{\tau}-\underline{U_{h}}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=1}^{N_{T}}\int_{t_{n-1}}^{t_{n}}\left\|\left(\frac{t-t_{n-1}}{\tau}-1\right)\Phi_{t}^{h}\Phi_{-t_{n}}^{h}U_{h}^{n}+\left(\frac{t_{n}-t}{\tau}\right)\Phi_{t}^{h}\Phi_{-t_{n-1}}^{h}U_{h}^{n-1}\right\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{n=1}^{N_{T}}\int_{t_{n-1}}^{t_{n}}\left\|\Phi_{t}^{h}\Phi_{-t_{n}}^{h}U_{h}^{n}-\Phi_{t}^{h}\Phi_{-t_{n-1}}^{h}U_{h}^{n-1}\right\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}^{2},

and so using (
5.7
) and (a
\delta\rightarrow 0
analogue of) (
5.33
) one obtains

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\|U_{h}^{\tau}-\underline{U_{h}}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}^{2}\leq C\tau\sum_{n=1}^{N_{T}}\|U_{h}^{n}-\overline{U_{h}^{n-1}}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}^{2}=C\tau^{3}\sum_{n=1}^{N_{T}}\|\partial^{\bullet}_{\tau}U_{h}^{n}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}^{2}\leq C\tau^{\frac{3}{2}}.

The
H^{1}
bound is similar, but uses the bound on
\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}\partial^{\bullet}_{\tau}U_{h}^{n}
(which one obtains in the
\delta\rightarrow 0
limit of (
5.18
)) instead.

The bound for (
5.42
) is more subtle.
To begin we compute, by a similar calculation to the above, that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\left\|(U_{h}^{\tau}-\underline{U_{h}})-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}(U_{h}^{\tau}-\underline{U_{h}})\right\|_{-h}^{2}\leq\tau^{2}\sum_{n=1}^{N_{T}}\int_{t_{n-1}}^{t_{n}}\left\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\left(\Phi_{t}^{h}\Phi_{-t_{n}}^{h}\partial^{\bullet}_{\tau}U_{h}^{n}-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}\Phi_{t}^{h}\Phi_{-t_{n}}^{h}\partial^{\bullet}_{\tau}U_{h}^{n}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t)))}^{2}.

The issue arises as we cannot immediately apply (
5.6
), analogously to the previous result, as
\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}
does not commute with pullbacks/pushforwards.
To mitigate this, the idea is to write

\displaystyle\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\Phi_{t}^{h}\Phi_{-t_{n}}^{h}=\Phi_{t}^{h}\Phi_{-t_{n}}^{h}\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}+\left(\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\Phi_{t}^{h}\Phi_{-t_{n}}^{h}-\Phi_{t}^{h}\Phi_{-t_{n}}^{h}\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\right),

where we have omitted the mean value terms for simplicity.
This first operator can now be dealt with by using (
5.6
), and (a
\delta\rightarrow 0
analogue of) (
5.34
).
For the bracketed term we obtain a similar bound by applying Lemma
A.5
with
z_{h}^{n}=\partial^{\bullet}_{\tau}U_{h}^{n}
.
We omit the calculations as they are tedious, and provide nothing that we have not remarked already — but a patient reader can indeed verify that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\left\|(U_{h}^{\tau}-\underline{U_{h}})-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}(U_{h}^{\tau}-\underline{U_{h}})\right\|_{-h}^{2}\leq C\tau^{3}\sum_{n=1}^{N_{T}}\left\|\partial^{\bullet}_{\tau}U_{h}^{n}-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{h}^{n}}\partial^{\bullet}_{\tau}U_{h}^{n}\right\|_{-h}^{2}+C\tau^{5}\sum_{n=1}^{N_{T}}\left\|\partial^{\bullet}_{\tau}U_{h}^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}^{2}.

(
5.42
) now follows from (
\delta\rightarrow 0
versions of) (
5.33
) and (
5.34
).
∎

Remark 5.16
.

It is a straightforward modification of this proof to also verify that

(5.43)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\left\|\partial^{\bullet}_{h}U_{h}^{\tau}-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.98003pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.26338pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.6363pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.45924pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}\partial^{\bullet}_{h}U_{h}^{\tau}\right\|_{-h}^{2}\leq C,

for a constant,
C
, independent of
h,\tau
.
We do not elaborate on further details on this proof.
Note also that by definition of
U_{h}^{\tau}
we can also write
\partial^{\bullet}_{h}U_{h}^{\tau}=\underline{\partial^{\bullet}_{\tau}U_{h}^{n}}(t)
, and we shall use these interchangeably.

We also have the following bounds on the mean values,

(5.44)

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}U_{h}^{\tau}-\int_{\Gamma_{h}(0)}U_{h,0}\right|\leq C\tau,\qquad\left|\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}\underline{U_{h}}-\int_{\Gamma_{h}(0)}U_{h,0}\right|\leq C\tau.

To see this we first observe that for
t\in(t_{n-1},t_{n}]
we may use (
5.7
) to see

\left|\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}\underline{U_{h}}-\int_{\Gamma_{h}(0)}U_{h,0}\right|=\left|\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}\underline{U_{h}}(t)-\int_{\Gamma_{h}^{n}}U_{h}^{n}\right|\leq C\tau\|U_{h}^{n}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}\leq C\tau.

The bound for
\left|\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}U_{h}^{\tau}-\int_{\Gamma_{h}(0)}U_{h,0}\right|
can be shown similarly by writing

\left|\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}U_{h}^{\tau}-\int_{\Gamma_{h}(0)}U_{h,0}\right|\leq\left(\frac{t-t_{n-1}}{\tau}\right)\left|\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}\underline{U_{h}^{n}}(t)-\int_{\Gamma_{h}(0)}U_{h,0}\right|+\left(\frac{t_{n}-t}{\tau}\right)\left|\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}\overline{U_{h}^{n-1}}(t)-\int_{\Gamma_{h}(0)}U_{h,0}\right|.

With these bounds at hand we can now show a fully discrete analogue of (
4.22
).

Lemma 5.17
.

The piecewise functions defined at the beginning of this section are such that, for sufficiently small
h,\tau,\delta
, with
\tau\leq\frac{\varepsilon^{3}}{2}
we have

(5.45)

\displaystyle\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}(\underline{U_{h}}-\underline{U_{h}^{\delta}})\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}^{2}\leq C\delta,

for some constant
C
independent of
h,\tau,\delta
.

Proof.

We begin by finding some estimates at the time discrete level.
Firstly we define
\widehat{U_{h}^{n,\delta}}=U_{h}^{n}-U_{h}^{n,\delta},\widehat{W_{h}^{n,\delta}}=W_{h}^{n}-W_{h}^{n,\delta}
and we have from (
5.1
), (
5.2
) and (
5.3
), (
5.4
) that

(5.46)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\tau}\left(\bar{m}_{h}(\widehat{U_{h}^{n,\delta}},\phi_{h}^{n})-\bar{m}_{h}(\widehat{U_{h}^{n-1,\delta}},\underline{\phi_{h}^{n}})\right)+a_{h}(\widehat{W_{h}^{n,\delta}},\phi_{h}^{n})=0,

(5.47)

\displaystyle\bar{m}_{h}(\widehat{W_{h}^{n,\delta}},\phi_{h}^{n})=\varepsilon a_{h}(\widehat{U_{h}^{n,\delta}},\phi_{h}^{n})+\frac{\theta}{2\varepsilon}\bar{m}_{h}(I_{h}f(U_{h}^{n})-I_{h}f^{\delta}(U_{h}^{n,\delta}),\phi_{h}^{n})-\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\bar{m}_{h}(\widehat{U_{h}^{n,\delta}},\phi_{h}^{n}),

for all
\phi_{h}^{n}\in S_{h}^{n}
.
We test (
5.46
) with
\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\widehat{U_{h}^{n,\delta}}
and (
5.47
) with
\widehat{U_{h}^{n,\delta}}
to obtain

\frac{1}{\tau}\left(\bar{m}_{h}(\widehat{U_{h}^{n,\delta}},\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\widehat{U_{h}^{n,\delta}})-\bar{m}_{h}(\widehat{U_{h}^{n-1,\delta}},\underline{\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\widehat{U_{h}^{n,\delta}}})\right)+\varepsilon a_{h}(\widehat{U_{h}^{n,\delta}},\widehat{U_{h}^{n,\delta}})+\frac{\theta}{2\varepsilon}\bar{m}_{h}(I_{h}f(U_{h}^{n})-I_{h}f^{\delta}(U_{h}^{n,\delta}),\widehat{U_{h}^{n,\delta}})\\
=\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\bar{m}_{h}(\widehat{U_{h}^{n,\delta}},\widehat{U_{h}^{n,\delta}}).

One can then verify (see also the proof of
[
35
, Proposition 3.1]
) that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\tau}\left(\bar{m}_{h}(\widehat{U_{h}^{n,\delta}},\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\widehat{U_{h}^{n,\delta}})-\bar{m}_{h}(\widehat{U_{h}^{n-1,\delta}},\underline{\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\widehat{U_{h}^{n,\delta}}})\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\tau}a_{h}(\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\widehat{U_{h}^{n,\delta}}-\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\widehat{U_{h,+}^{n-1,\delta}},\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\widehat{U_{h}^{n,\delta}})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\tau}\left(\left\|\widehat{U_{h}^{n,\delta}}\right\|_{-h}^{2}-\left\|\widehat{U_{h,+}^{n-1,\delta}}\right\|_{-h}^{2}+\left\|\widehat{U_{h}^{n,\delta}}-\widehat{U_{h,+}^{n-1,\delta}}\right\|_{-h}^{2}\right).

Using this, we find that

(5.48)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\left\|\widehat{U_{h}^{n,\delta}}\right\|_{-h}^{2}-\left\|\widehat{U_{h}^{n-1,\delta}}\right\|_{-h}^{2}+\left\|\widehat{U_{h}^{n,\delta}}-\widehat{U_{h,+}^{n-1,\delta}}\right\|_{-h}^{2}+2\varepsilon\tau\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}\widehat{U_{h}^{n,\delta}}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}^{2}\\
&+\frac{\tau\theta}{\varepsilon}\bar{m}_{h}(I_{h}f^{\delta}(U_{h}^{n})-I_{h}f^{\delta}(U_{h}^{n,\delta}),\widehat{U_{h}^{n,\delta}})=\left[\left\|U_{h,+}^{n-1,\delta}\right\|_{-h}^{2}-\left\|\widehat{U_{h}^{n-1,\delta}}\right\|_{-h}^{2}\right]+\frac{2\tau}{\varepsilon}\|\widehat{U_{h}^{n,\delta}}\|_{h,t_{n}}^{2}\\
&+\frac{\tau\theta}{\varepsilon}\bar{m}_{h}(I_{h}f^{\delta}(U_{h}^{n})-I_{h}f(U_{h}^{n}),\widehat{U_{h}^{n,\delta}}).\end{aligned}

By mirroring the proof of
[
35
, Proposition 3.1]
(modulo the use of mass-lumping) one can show that

\left|\left\|U_{h,+}^{n-1,\delta}\right\|_{-h}^{2}-\left\|\widehat{U_{h}^{n-1,\delta}}\right\|_{-h}^{2}\right|\leq C\tau\left\|\widehat{U_{h}^{n-1,\delta}}\right\|_{-h}^{2},

for a constant,
C
, independent of
h,\tau,\delta
.
Likewise we observe that

\frac{2\tau}{\varepsilon}\|\widehat{U_{h}^{n,\delta}}\|_{h,t_{n}}^{2}=\frac{2\tau}{\varepsilon}a_{h}(\widehat{U_{h}^{n,\delta}},\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\widehat{U_{h}^{n,\delta}})\leq\varepsilon\tau\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}\widehat{U_{h}^{n,\delta}}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}^{2}+\frac{\tau}{\varepsilon^{3}}\left\|\widehat{U_{h}^{n,\delta}}\right\|_{-h}^{2}.

Now one argues as in Theorem
3.1
, where we omit the details, to find that (
5.48
) yields

(5.49)

\left\|\widehat{U_{h}^{n,\delta}}\right\|_{-h}^{2}-\left\|\widehat{U_{h}^{n-1,\delta}}\right\|_{-h}^{2}+\left\|\widehat{U_{h}^{n,\delta}}-\widehat{U_{h,+}^{n-1,\delta}}\right\|_{-h}^{2}+2\varepsilon\tau\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}\widehat{U_{h}^{n,\delta}}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}^{2}\leq\frac{\tau}{\varepsilon^{3}}\left\|\widehat{U_{h}^{n,\delta}}\right\|_{-h}^{2}\\
+C\tau\left\|\widehat{U_{h}^{n-1,\delta}}\right\|_{-h}^{2}+C\tau\delta\|I_{h}f(U_{h}^{n})\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}^{2}.

Hence by summing over
n
, noting that
\frac{\tau}{\varepsilon^{3}}\leq\frac{1}{2}
and using (a
\delta\rightarrow 0
analogue of) (
5.37
), we may use a discrete Grönwall inequality (noting that
\widehat{U_{h}^{0,\delta}}=0
) to find

\varepsilon\tau\sum_{n=1}^{N_{T}}\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}\widehat{U_{h}^{n,\delta}}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}^{2}\leq C\delta,

and we use this to obtain the desired bound.
One finds from this and (
5.6
)

\displaystyle\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}(\underline{U_{h}}-\underline{U_{h}^{\delta}})\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}^{2}

\displaystyle=\varepsilon\sum_{n=1}^{N_{T}}\int_{t_{n-1}}^{t_{n}}\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}\Phi_{t}^{h}\Phi^{h}_{-t_{n}}\widehat{U_{h}^{n,\delta}}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon\tau\sum_{n=1}^{N_{T}}\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}\widehat{U_{h}^{n,\delta}}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}^{2}\leq C\delta.

∎

There is now one more error bound to show, from which we infer our final error bound.
This is the content of the following lemma.

Lemma 5.18
.

Let
U_{h}^{\delta}
be the solution from (
4.4
), (
4.5
), and
\underline{U_{h}^{\delta}},U_{h}^{\tau,\delta}
the piecewise functions defined above.
Then for sufficiently small
h,\tau,\delta
,

(5.50)

\displaystyle\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}(U_{h}^{\delta}-\underline{U_{h}^{\delta}})\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}^{2}+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\|U_{h}^{\delta}-U_{h}^{\tau,\delta}\right\|_{-h}^{2}\leq C\left(\tau+\frac{\tau^{2}}{\delta^{2}}\right),

for some constant
C
independent of
h,\tau,\delta
.

Proof.

As usual we define some shorthand notation to be used throughout,

\displaystyle E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}:=U_{h}^{\delta}-U_{h}^{\tau,\delta},\qquad E_{u,h}^{\delta}:=U_{h}^{\delta}-\underline{U_{h}^{\delta}},\qquad{E_{w,h}^{\delta}}:=W_{h}^{\delta}-\underline{W_{h}^{\delta}},

from which it is clear that
E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}-E_{u,h}^{\delta}=\underline{U_{h}^{\delta}}-U_{h}^{\tau,\delta}
.
Unlike the error analysis on a stationary domain (see for instance
[
7
]
) these functions
do not
have vanishing mean value.
As such we also define the functions

\displaystyle\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}}:=E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta},\qquad\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\delta}}:=E_{u,h}^{\delta}-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}E_{u,h}^{\delta},

to which we may apply the inverse Laplacians from Appendix
A
.
Then subtracting the regularised version of (
5.3
) from (
4.4
), (
4.5
) we obtain

(5.51)

\bar{m}_{h}(\partial^{\bullet}_{h}E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta},\phi_{h})+g_{h}(I_{h}(E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}\phi_{h}),1)+a_{h}(E_{w,h}^{\delta},\phi_{h})=g_{h}\left(I_{h}\left(\underline{U_{h}^{\delta}}\phi_{h}\right),1\right)-g_{h}(I_{h}(U_{h}^{\tau,\delta}\phi_{h}),1)\\
-\sum_{k=1}^{3}D_{k}^{\delta}(\phi_{h}),

and

(5.52)

\bar{m}_{h}(E_{w,h}^{\delta},\phi_{h})=\varepsilon a_{h}(E_{u,h}^{\delta},\phi_{h})+\frac{\theta}{2\varepsilon}\bar{m}_{h}\left(I_{h}f^{\delta}(U_{h}^{\delta})-I_{h}f^{\delta}(\underline{U_{h}^{\delta}}),\phi_{h}\right)-\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\bar{m}_{h}(E_{u,h}^{\delta},\phi_{h})\\
-\sum_{k=4}^{7}D_{k}^{\delta}(\phi_{h}).

We then test (
5.51
) with
\phi_{h}=\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\delta}}
, so that

(5.53)

\displaystyle\bar{m}_{h}\left(\partial^{\bullet}_{h}E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta},\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}}\right)+g_{h}\left(I_{h}\left(E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}}\right),1\right)+a_{h}\left(E_{w,h}^{\delta},\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\delta}}\right)=\sum_{k=1}^{4}J_{k},

where

\displaystyle J_{1}:=\bar{m}_{h}\left(\partial^{\bullet}_{h}E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta},\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\left(\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}}-\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\delta}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle J_{2}:=g_{h}\left(I_{h}\left(E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\left(\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}}-\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\delta}}\right)\right),1\right),

\displaystyle J_{3}:=g_{h}\left(I_{h}\left(\underline{U_{h}^{\delta}}\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\delta}}\right),1\right)-g_{h}\left(I_{h}(U_{h}^{\tau,\delta}\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\delta}}),1\right),

\displaystyle J_{4}:=-\sum_{i=1}^{3}D_{i}^{\delta}\left(\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\delta}}\right).

Then, as usual, we observe from (
5.52
) and the definition of
\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}
that

a_{h}\left(E_{w,h}^{\delta},\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\delta}}\right)=\bar{m}_{h}\left(E_{w,h}^{\delta},\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\delta}}\right)=\varepsilon a_{h}(E_{u,h}^{\delta},E_{u,h}^{\delta})+\frac{\theta}{2\varepsilon}\bar{m}_{h}\left(I_{h}f^{\delta}(U_{h}^{\delta})-I_{h}f^{\delta}(\underline{U_{h}^{\delta}}),\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\delta}}\right)\\
-\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\bar{m}_{h}\left(E_{u,h}^{\delta},\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\delta}}\right)-\sum_{i=4}^{7}D_{i}^{\delta}\left(\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\delta}}\right).

Hence using this in (
5.53
) one has

(5.54)

\bar{m}_{h}\left(\partial^{\bullet}_{h}E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta},\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}}\right)+g_{h}\left(I_{h}(E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}}),1\right)+\varepsilon\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}E_{u,h}^{\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}^{2}\\
+\frac{\theta}{2\varepsilon}\bar{m}_{h}\left(I_{h}f^{\delta}(U_{h}^{\delta})-I_{h}f^{\delta}(\underline{U_{h}^{\delta}}),\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\delta}}\right)=\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\bar{m}_{h}\left(E_{u,h}^{\delta},\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\delta}}\right)+\sum_{k=1}^{5}J_{k},

where

\displaystyle J_{5}:=\sum_{i=4}^{7}D_{i}^{\delta}\left(\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\delta}}\right).

To begin we firstly write

\bar{m}_{h}\left(\partial^{\bullet}_{h}E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta},\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}}\right)+g_{h}\left(I_{h}(E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}}),1\right)=\bar{m}_{h}\left(\partial^{\bullet}_{h}\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}},\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}}\right)+g_{h}\left(I_{h}(\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}}\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}}),1\right)\\
+\bar{m}_{h}\left(\partial^{\bullet}_{h}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta},\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}}\right)+g_{h}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta},\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}}\right),

where we note that
I_{h}(\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}})=\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}}
.
Now from Proposition
2.17
, Lemma
2.29
, and the definition of
\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}
, it is a straightforward calculation to see that

\bar{m}_{h}\left(\partial^{\bullet}_{h}E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta},\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}}\right)+g_{h}\left(I_{h}(E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}}),1\right)=\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}}\right\|_{-h}^{2}-\frac{1}{2}b_{h}\left(\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}},\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}}\right)\\
+\bar{m}_{h}\left(\partial^{\bullet}_{h}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta},\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}}\right)+g_{h}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta},\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}}\right)

We use this, and the monotonicity of
f^{\delta}
, in (
5.54
) to finally see that

(5.55)

\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}}\right\|_{-h}^{2}+\varepsilon\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}E_{u,h}^{\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}^{2}\leq\frac{\theta}{2\varepsilon}\bar{m}_{h}\left(I_{h}f^{\delta}(U_{h}^{\delta})-I_{h}f^{\delta}(\underline{U_{h}^{\delta}}),\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}E_{u,h}^{\delta}\right)\\
+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\bar{m}_{h}\left(E_{u,h}^{\delta},\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\delta}}\right)+\frac{1}{2}b_{h}\left(\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}},\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}}\right)\\
+\left|\bar{m}_{h}\left(\partial^{\bullet}_{h}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta},\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}}\right)\right|+\left|g_{h}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta},\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}}\right)\right|+\sum_{i=1}^{5}|J_{i}|,

and all that remains is to bound these terms accordingly.

We begin with the useful observation that
\left|\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}E_{u,h}^{\delta}\right|\leq C\tau
.
To see this is true we can calculate

\left|\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}E_{u,h}^{\delta}\right|=\frac{1}{|\Gamma_{h}(t)|}\left|\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}U_{h}^{\delta}(t)-\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}\underline{U_{h}^{\delta}}(t)\right|=\frac{1}{|\Gamma_{h}(t)|}\left|\int_{\Gamma_{h}(0)}U_{h,0}-\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}\underline{U_{h}^{\delta}}(t)\right|\leq C\tau,

where we have used (a
\delta\neq 0
version of) (
5.44
).
A similar calculation yields
\left|\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}\right|\leq C\tau
.
Hence using this observation, (
3.5
), and the Poincaré inequality we find

(5.56)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \left|\frac{\theta}{2\varepsilon}\bar{m}_{h}\left(I_{h}f^{\delta}(U_{h}^{\delta})-I_{h}f^{\delta}(\underline{U_{h}^{\delta}}),\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}E_{u,h}^{\delta}\right)\right|&\leq\frac{C\tau}{\delta}\|E_{u,h}^{\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}\\
&\leq\frac{C\tau}{\delta}\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}E_{u,h}^{\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}+\frac{C\tau}{\delta}\left|\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}E_{u,h}^{\delta}\right|\\
&\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{4}\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}E_{u,h}^{\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}^{2}+\frac{C\tau^{2}}{\delta^{2}},\end{aligned}

where we have used Young’s inequality for the final inequality.
Similarly one finds that

(5.57)

\displaystyle\left|g_{h}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta},\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}}\right)\right|\leq C\tau\left\|\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}}\right\|_{-h},

where we have also used (
2.24
) and the Poincaré inequality.
Since
\partial^{\bullet}_{h}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}
depends only on
t
we find

\bar{m}_{h}\left(\partial^{\bullet}_{h}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta},\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}}\right)=0.

Next we use the definition of
\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}
and (
A.4
) to find

(5.58)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \frac{1}{\varepsilon}\bar{m}_{h}\left(E_{u,h}^{\delta},\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\delta}}\right)&=\frac{1}{\varepsilon}a_{h}\left(E_{u,h}^{\delta},\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\delta}}\right)\\
&\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{4}\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}E_{u,h}^{\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}^{2}+C\left\|\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\delta}}\right\|_{-h}^{2},\end{aligned}

and we observe that

\left\|\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\delta}}\right\|_{-h}\leq\left\|\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}}\right\|_{-h}+\left\|\underline{U_{h}^{\delta}}-U_{h}^{\tau,\delta}-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}\left(\underline{U_{h}^{\delta}}-U_{h}^{\tau,\delta}\right)\right\|_{-h},

where this rightmost term will be bounded by using (a
\delta\neq 0
analogue of) (
5.42
).
From our smoothness assumptions on
V
we find that, as usual,

(5.59)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}b_{h}\left(\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}},\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}}\right)\leq C\left\|\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}}\right\|_{-h}^{2}.

All that remains is to bound the
J_{i}
terms.
To bound
J_{1}
we first split
\partial^{\bullet}_{h}E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}=\partial^{\bullet}_{h}U_{h}^{\delta}-\partial^{\bullet}_{h}U_{h}^{\tau,\delta}
and observe that

\displaystyle J_{1}

\displaystyle=\bar{m}_{h}\left(\partial^{\bullet}_{h}U_{h}^{\delta},\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}(\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}}-\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\delta}})\right)+\bar{m}_{h}\left(\underline{\partial^{\bullet}_{\tau}U_{h}^{n,\delta}}(t),\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}(\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}}-\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\delta}})\right)

\displaystyle=\bar{m}_{h}\left(\partial^{\bullet}_{h}U_{h}^{\delta},\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}(\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}}-\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\delta}})\right)+\bar{m}_{h}\left(\underline{\partial^{\bullet}_{\tau}U_{h}^{n,\delta}}(t)-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}\underline{\partial^{\bullet}_{\tau}U_{h}^{n,\delta}}(t),\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}(\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}}-\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\delta}})\right),

where we have used the fact that
\partial^{\bullet}_{h}U_{h}^{\tau,\delta}=\underline{\partial^{\bullet}_{\tau}U_{h}^{n,\delta}}(t)
.
Now we use the definitions of
\|\cdot\|_{H^{-1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}
and
\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}
to see

\displaystyle|J_{1}|\leq C\left(\|\partial^{\bullet}_{h}U_{h}^{\delta}\|_{H^{-1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}+\left\|\underline{\partial^{\bullet}_{\tau}U_{h}^{n,\delta}}(t)-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}\underline{\partial^{\bullet}_{\tau}U_{h}^{n,\delta}}(t)\right\|_{-h}\right)\left\|\underline{U_{h}^{\delta}}-U_{h}^{\tau,\delta}-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}\left(\underline{U_{h}^{\delta}}-U_{h}^{\tau,\delta}\right)\right\|_{-h}

where we now use Hölder’s inequality, Lemma
4.5
, and (
\delta\neq 0
analogues of) (
5.42
), (
5.43
) to see that

(5.60)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}|J_{1}|\leq C\tau\left(\int_{0}^{T}\|\partial^{\bullet}_{h}U_{h}^{\delta}\|_{H^{-1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\left\|\underline{\partial^{\bullet}_{\tau}U_{h}^{n,\delta}}(t)-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}\underline{\partial^{\bullet}_{\tau}U_{h}^{n,\delta}}(t)\right\|_{-h}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C\tau.

To bound
J_{2}
we firstly write

\displaystyle J_{2}=g_{h}\left(I_{h}\left(\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}}\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\left(\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}}-\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\delta}}\right)\right),1\right)+g_{h}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta},\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}\left(\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}}-\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\delta}}\right)\right).

Now using the smoothness of
V
, the bound on
\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}
, and Poincaré’s inequality we have

\displaystyle|J_{2}|\leq C\|\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}\left\|\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}}-\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\delta}}\right\|_{-h}+C\tau\left\|\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}}-\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\delta}}\right\|_{-h}

Hence we now use (
A.4
), Young’s inequality, (
5.42
), and our CFL condition to see that

(5.61)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \int_{0}^{T}|J_{2}|&\leq C\tau^{2}+\frac{C}{h}\int_{0}^{T}\left\|\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}}\right\|_{-h}\left\|\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}}-\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\delta}}\right\|_{-h}\\
&\leq C\int_{0}^{T}\left\|\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}}\right\|_{-h}^{2}+C\tau^{2}+\underbrace{\frac{C\tau^{2}}{h^{2}}}_{\leq C\tau}.\end{aligned}

To bound
|J_{3}|
we find that using Poincaré’s inequality

\displaystyle|J_{3}|\leq C\|\underline{U_{h}^{\delta}}-U^{\tau,\delta}_{h}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}\left\|\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\delta}}\right\|_{-h}

from which we find that

\displaystyle|J_{3}|\leq C\|\underline{U_{h}^{\delta}}-U^{\tau,\delta}_{h}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}\left\|\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}}\right\|_{-h}+C\|\underline{U_{h}^{\delta}}-U^{\tau,\delta}_{h}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}\left\|\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}}-\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\delta}}\right\|_{-h}

and hence we use (
5.41
), (
5.42
), and Young’s inequality to see that

(5.62)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}|J_{3}|\leq C\tau^{\frac{3}{2}}+C\int_{0}^{T}\left\|\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}}\right\|_{-h}^{2}.

From Lemma
5.14
one immediately has that (after using the Poincaré inequality where appropriate)

\displaystyle|J_{4}|

\displaystyle\leq C\tau\left(\|U_{h}^{n,\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}+\|\partial^{\bullet}_{\tau}U_{h}^{n,\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}+\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}W_{h}^{n,\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}\right)\left\|\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\delta}}\right\|_{-h}

\displaystyle\leq C\tau\left(\|U_{h}^{n,\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}+\|\partial^{\bullet}_{\tau}U_{h}^{n,\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}+\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}W_{h}^{n,\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}\right)\left\|\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}}\right\|_{-h}

\displaystyle+C\tau\left(\|U_{h}^{n,\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}+\|\partial^{\bullet}_{\tau}U_{h}^{n,\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}+\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}W_{h}^{n,\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}\right)\left\|\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}}-\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\delta}}\right\|_{-h},

and similarly that

\displaystyle|J_{5}|\leq C\tau\left(\|U_{h}^{n,\delta}\|_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}+\|I_{h}f^{\delta}(U_{h}^{n,\delta})\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}+\|W_{h}^{n,\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}\right)\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}E_{u,h}^{\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}.

Hence we integrate over
[0,T]
and use (
5.18
), (
5.33
), (
5.37
), (
5.42
), and Young’s inequality to see that

(5.63)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}|J_{4}|\leq C\tau^{\frac{3}{2}}+C\int_{0}^{T}\left\|\widetilde{E_{u,h}^{\tau,\delta}}\right\|_{-h}^{2},

(5.64)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}|J_{5}|\leq C\tau^{2}+\frac{\varepsilon}{4}\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}E^{\delta}_{u,h}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}^{2}.

One then concludes by integrating in time, using (
5.56
)–(
5.64
) in (
5.55
) and Grönwall’s inequality.
We omit further details.
∎

We are now in a position to prove our final error bound.

Theorem 5.19
.

Let
(u,w)
be the unique solution of (
3.1
),(
3.2
) and
\underline{U_{h}}
be defined as above.
Then for
h,\tau
sufficiently small we have

(5.65)

\displaystyle\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}(u^{-\ell}-\underline{U_{h}})\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}^{2}\leq C\left(\tau+h^{\frac{4}{3}}\log\left(\frac{1}{h}\right)+\frac{\tau^{2}}{h^{\frac{8}{3}}}\right),

for
C
a constant independent of
h,\tau.

Proof.

This follows by using the splitting

u^{-\ell}-\underline{U_{h}}=[u^{-\ell}-(u^{\delta})^{-\ell}]+[(u^{\delta})^{-\ell}-U_{h}^{\delta}]+[U_{h}^{\delta}-\underline{U_{h}^{\delta}}]-[\underline{U_{h}^{\delta}}-\underline{U_{h}}],

and the error bounds (
3.7
), (
4.23
), (
4.27
), (
5.45
), (
5.50
) where we have chosen
\delta=C(p)h^{p}
, for a small constant
C(p)
, and for maximal order we take
p=\frac{4}{3}
.
Note that taking
\tau\leq Ch^{2}
, in accordance with our CFL condition, we obtain the same order error bound as in in the semi-discrete case.
∎

Remark 5.20
.

(1)

As discussed in the semi-discrete error, this will hold for any
\tilde{U}_{h,0}\in\mathcal{I}_{h,0}
such that

\int_{\Gamma_{h}(0)}\tilde{U}_{h,0}=\int_{\Gamma_{0}}u_{0},\quad\mathrm{and}\quad\|\Pi_{h}u_{0}-\tilde{U}_{h,0}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(0))}\leq Ch^{\frac{2}{3}}\log\left(\frac{1}{h}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},

for some constant
C
independent of
h
.
Moreover, from (
5.41
) we also find that

\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}(u^{-\ell}-U_{h}^{\tau})\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}^{2}\leq C\left(\tau+h^{\frac{4}{3}}\log\left(\frac{1}{h}\right)+\frac{\tau^{2}}{h^{\frac{8}{3}}}\right).

(2)

It may be possible to adapt arguments from
[
9
]
to improve this bound, but we do not consider this here.

6.
Numerical experiments

In this section we present some numerical results for an implementation of the fully discrete scheme (
5.1
), (
5.2
).
We may express the fully discrete scheme in a block matrix form as

\begin{pmatrix}\bar{M}^{n}&\tau A^{n}\\
-\varepsilon A^{n}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\bar{M}^{n}&\bar{M}^{n}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\alpha^{n}\\
\beta^{n}\end{pmatrix}-\frac{\theta}{2\varepsilon}\begin{pmatrix}0\\
\bar{M}^{n}f(\alpha^{n})\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\bar{M}^{n-1}\alpha^{n-1}\\
0\end{pmatrix},

as we have throughout the paper.
Here
\alpha^{n},\beta^{n}
are such that

U_{h}^{n}=\sum_{j=1}^{N_{h}}\alpha_{j}^{n}\phi_{j}^{n},\quad W_{h}^{n}=\sum_{j=1}^{N_{h}}\beta_{j}^{n}\phi_{j}^{n}.

One cannot solve this scheme immediately by use of standard Newton methods, as one has the constraint that
\alpha^{n}\in(-1,1)^{N_{h}}
.
One could remedy this by considering a Newton scheme with variable stepsizes, see for instance
[
23
]
, or in our case by using a variant of the nonsmooth Newton solver in
[
43
]
.
The corresponding linear system to solve is solved by an exact solver based on LU decomposition with pivoting.
We omit further details on the implementation.

6.1.
Dynamics on an expanding torus

Here we consider the torus given by the level set equation

\displaystyle\left(\sqrt{x^{2}+y^{2}}-0.75-t\right)^{2}+z^{2}-0.25^{2}=0,

which one can verify has
\nabla_{\Gamma}\cdot V>0
.
We choose the initial data to be
u_{0}(x,y,z)=0.9x\cos\left(\frac{\pi y}{2}\right)
, which we observe has vanishing mean value.
Moreover we take
\varepsilon=0.1
,
\theta=0.4
and
T=0.6
.
The mesh here consists of 6016 elements, and
\tau=5\cdot 10^{-5}
.

Figure 1
.
Plot of the Ginzburg-Landau functional on an expanding torus over
[0,0.6]
.

We note that the Ginzburg-Landau functional as plotted in Figure
1
is non-monotonic.
Heuristically can explain this as a competition of two contrary behaviours.
Firstly for small
\varepsilon
the Ginzburg-Landau functional (
1.3
) is approximately the length of some curve
\gamma(t)\subset\Gamma(t)
which evolves via some evolving surface analogue of the Mullins-Sekerka flow obtained in the sharp interface limit
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
— we refer the reader to a similar discussion in
[
35
, Section 6.3]
.
This should, and for a stationary domain does, shrink the curve till it converges to some curve of a fixed length.
On the other hand, the surface
\Gamma(t)
is expanding, and so sufficiently fast expansion of
\Gamma(t)
would lead to the length of
\gamma(t)
increasing.
Thus the two regimes seen in Figure
1
are justified by these two behaviours respectively.

(a)

t=0.

(b)

t=0.1.

(c)

t=0.2.

(d)

t=0.3.

Figure 2
.
Evolution of
u
on an expanding torus.
Regions of blue correspond to a negative quantity, and red a positive quantity.

6.2.
Dynamics on a shrinking torus

Here we consider a shrinking torus, given by the level set equation

\left(\sqrt{x^{2}+y^{2}}-0.75\right)^{2}+z^{2}-(0.25-0.25t)^{2}=0,

to investigate the necessity of our assumption that
\nabla_{\Gamma}\cdot V\geq 0
.
It is a straightforward calculation to verify that this level set is such that
\nabla_{\Gamma}\cdot V<0
.
We choose the same initial data and values for
\varepsilon,\theta,\tau
as in the previous experiment.
The theory from
[
14
,
15
]
shows that a solution exists for
t\in[0,1)
, and this experiment investigates whether any numerical issues arise when the true solution is defined.
We find that there seems to be no numerical issues on this small time interval, which indicates some hope for extending our numerical analysis to evolving surfaces without the condition that
\nabla_{\Gamma}\cdot V>0
.
We observe in Figure
3
that the Ginzburg-Landau functional is monotonically decreasing here, and this follows the same heuristic argument as we saw for the expanding case.

Figure 3
.
Plot of the Ginzburg-Landau functional on a shrinking torus over
[0,0.6]
.

We also include some examples of the evolution of
u
on this shrinking domain, Figure
4
, which shows how the behaviour of the solution is vastly different to an expanding domain — see Figure
2
.

(a)

t=0.

(b)

t=0.1.

(c)

t=0.2.

(d)

t=0.3.

Figure 4
.
Evolution of
u
on a shrinking torus.
Regions of blue correspond to a negative quantity, and red a positive quantity.

6.3.
Experimental order of convergence on an expanding sphere

Here we compute an experimental order of convergence (EOC) for the
H^{1}
error of the scheme (
5.1
), (
5.2
) on an expanding sphere
6
6

6

This example is the unit sphere evolving by inverse mean curvature flow (
V_{N}=\frac{1}{H}
) which fits our assumptions on the expansion of the surface as discussed previously.
, given by the level set equation

x^{2}+y^{2}+z^{2}-e^{t}=0.

The EOC is computed by solving the equation on a coarse mesh, and prolonging this coarse solution onto a finer mesh.
This process is analogous to lifting.
We obtain an error by approximating the true solution with a fine solution (
\tau=10^{-5},h\approx 4.023559\cdot 10^{-2}
), as the exact solution is not known.
We choose the timestep sizes to be
\mathcal{O}(h^{2})
, in accordance with our CFL condition, and this also avoids a bottleneck in the error.
We observe that the EOC is larger than predicted by Theorem
5.19
.
This is not surprising as our proof relies on using the regularised potential, which introduces a bottleneck that practical schemes will not be limited by.

Here the parameters
\varepsilon,\theta,T
are chosen as
\varepsilon=0.1,\theta=0.5,T=0.1
, and the initial data is given by
u_{0}(x,y,z)=0.5x
.

h

Table 1
.
Table of EOC for the expanding sphere.

We note that in this numerical experiment the mesh started acute (for all levels of refinement) but immediately lost this property.
Despite this the solution remained numerically stable and appears to be have an optimal order of convergence in the
H^{1}
seminorm.

Appendix A
Inverse Laplacians

In this appendix we discuss several notions of inverse Laplacians and related inequalities.

Definition A.1
.

Let
z\in H^{-1}(\Gamma(t))
with
m_{*}(z,1)=0
.
We define the inverse Laplacian of
z
,
\mathcal{G}z
, to be the unique solution of

a(\mathcal{G}z,\eta)=m_{*}(z,\eta),\qquad\int_{\Gamma(t)}\mathcal{G}z=0,

for all
\eta\in H^{1}(\Gamma(t))
.

We also recall the following result from
[
14
,
32
]
.

Lemma A.2
.

If
z\in L^{2}_{H^{1}}\cap H^{1}_{H^{-1}}
and
m_{*}(z,1)=0
, then
\mathcal{G}z
is well defined and
\mathcal{G}z\in H^{1}_{H^{1}}
.

We also require three different notions of a discrete inverse Laplacian on
\Gamma_{h}(t)
.

Definition A.3
.

Let
z_{h}\in L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))
such that
\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}z_{h}=0
.

(1)

We define the discrete inverse Laplacian,
\mathcal{G}_{h}z_{h}\in H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))
, to be the unique solution of

a_{h}(\mathcal{G}_{h}z_{h},\eta_{h})=m_{h}(z_{h},\eta_{h}),\qquad\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}\mathcal{G}_{h}z_{h}=0,

for all
\eta_{h}\in H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))
.

(2)

Similarly for
z_{h}\in S_{h}(t)
, we define the inverse Laplacian on shape functions to be the unique solution,
\mathcal{G}_{S_{h}}z_{h}\in S_{h}(t)
, of

a_{h}(\mathcal{G}_{S_{h}}z_{h},\phi_{h})=m_{h}(z_{h},\phi_{h}),\qquad\int_{\Gamma_{h}}\mathcal{G}_{S_{h}}z_{h}=0,

for all
\phi_{h}\in S_{h}(t)
.

(3)

Lastly for
z_{h}\in S_{h}(t)
, we define a mass-lumped inverse Laplacian as the unique solution,
\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}z_{h}\in S_{h}(t)
, of

a_{h}\left(\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}z_{h},\phi_{h}\right)=\bar{m}_{h}(z_{h},\phi_{h}),\qquad\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}z_{h},=0,

for all
\phi_{h}\in S_{h}(t)
, provided that
z_{h}\in C^{0}(\Gamma_{h}(t))
.

Each of these operators gives rise to an corresponding norm, given by

\displaystyle\|z\|_{-1}^{2}:=a(\mathcal{G}z,\mathcal{G}z),\qquad\|z_{h}\|_{-1,h}^{2}:=a_{h}(\mathcal{G}_{h}z_{h},\mathcal{G}_{h}z_{h}),

\displaystyle\left\|z_{h}\right\|_{S_{h}}^{2}:=a_{h}(\mathcal{G}_{S_{h}}z_{h},\mathcal{G}_{S_{h}}z_{h}),\qquad\left\|z_{h}\right\|_{-h}^{2}:=a_{h}\left(\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}z_{h},\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}z_{h}\right).

It is clear that these are well defined norms due to the Poincaré inequality and the condition on the mean value.
We will use the inverse Laplacian to establish bounds in
H^{-1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))
, since

(A.1)

\|z_{h}\|_{H^{-1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}=\sup_{\eta_{h}\in H^{1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))\backslash\{0\}}\frac{|m_{h}(z_{h},\eta_{h})|}{\|\eta_{h}\|_{H^{1}{(\Gamma_{h}(t))}}}\leq\|z_{h}\|_{-1,h},

for
z_{h}\in L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))
with
\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}z_{h}=0
.

We relate the second and third inverse Laplacians through the inequality,

(A.2)

\displaystyle\left\|(\mathcal{G}_{h}-\mathcal{G}_{S_{h}})z_{h}\right\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}\leq Ch^{2}\|z_{h}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}.

This can be seen as an error bound for linear finite elements solving Laplace’s equation posed on
\Gamma_{h}(t)
(see
[
26
]
).
Furthermore we have the following inequalities.

Lemma A.4
.

Let
z_{h}\in S_{h}(t)
with
\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}z_{h}=0
, then we have,

(A.3)

\displaystyle C_{1}h^{2}\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}z_{h}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}\leq C_{2}h\|z_{h}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}\leq\left\|z_{h}\right\|_{S_{h}}\leq\|z_{h}\|_{-1,h}\leq C_{3}\left\|z_{h}\right\|_{S_{h}}.

(A.4)

\displaystyle C_{4}h^{2}\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}z_{h}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}\leq C_{5}h\left\|z_{h}\right\|_{h,t}\leq C_{6}\left\|z_{h}\right\|_{-h}\leq C_{7}\left\|z_{h}\right\|_{S_{h}}\leq C_{8}\left\|z_{h}\right\|_{-h}.

Proof.

The first inequality in the chain follows by Poincaré’s inequality and an inverse inequality (see
[
13
]
).
The second inequality comes from

h^{2}\|z_{h}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}^{2}=h^{2}a_{h}\left(\mathcal{G}_{S_{h}}z_{h},z_{h}\right)\leq\frac{1}{\lambda}\left\|z_{h}\right\|_{S_{h}}^{2}+\frac{\lambda h^{4}}{4}\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}z_{h}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}^{2}\\
\leq\frac{1}{\lambda}\left\|z_{h}\right\|_{S_{h}}^{2}+\frac{C\lambda h^{2}}{4}\|z_{h}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}^{2},

where we have used Young’s inequality and the first inequality.
The inequality follows by taking
\lambda
sufficiently small so that
\frac{C\lambda}{4}<1
.
The third inequality comes from

a_{h}\left(\mathcal{G}_{S_{h}}z_{h},\mathcal{G}_{S_{h}}z_{h}\right)=m_{h}\left(z_{h},\mathcal{G}_{S_{h}}z_{h}\right)=a_{h}\left(\mathcal{G}_{h}z_{h},\mathcal{G}_{S_{h}}z_{h}\right).

The final inequality then follows from

\|z_{h}\|_{-1,h}^{2}-\left\|z_{h}\right\|_{S_{h}}^{2}=m_{h}\left(z_{h},(\mathcal{G}_{h}-\mathcal{G}_{S_{h}})z_{h}\right)\leq Ch^{2}\|z_{h}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}^{2}\leq C\left\|z_{h}\right\|_{S_{h}}^{2},

where we have used (
A.2
) and the second inequality.
The proof of (
A.4
) is more or less identical, and hence omitted.
∎

We note that (
A.2
), (
A.3
), (
A.4
) show that for
z_{h}\in S_{h}(t)
with
\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}z_{h}=0
we may control
\|z_{h}\|_{H^{-1}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}
by
\left\|z_{h}\right\|_{S_{h}}
or
\left\|z_{h}\right\|_{-h}
.

Lastly we note that each of these operators changes in time as the geometry of
\Gamma(t)
varies.
As such, we will need the following commutator bound which compares two notions of an inverse Laplacian on
\Gamma_{h}(t)
.
This result will be required in the proof of Lemma
5.15
.

Lemma A.5
.

Let
z_{h}^{n}\in S_{h}^{n}
and
t\in[t_{n-1},t_{n}]
.
Then for

\displaystyle z_{h,0}^{n}:=z_{h}^{n}-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.98003pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.26338pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.6363pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.45924pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{h}^{n}}z_{h}^{n}\in S_{h}^{n},

\displaystyle z_{h,0}^{t}:=\Phi_{t}^{h}\Phi_{-t_{n}}^{h}z_{h}^{n}-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.98003pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.26338pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.6363pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.45924pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}\Phi_{t}^{h}\Phi_{-t_{n}}^{h}z_{h}^{n}\in S_{h}(t),

one has that

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}\left(\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}z_{h,0}^{t}-\Phi_{t}^{h}\Phi_{-t_{n-1}}^{h}\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}z_{h,0}^{n}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}\leq C\tau\|z_{h}^{n}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})},

for a constant
C
independent of
\tau,h
.

Proof.

We begin by noting that

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}\left(\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}z_{h,0}^{t}-\Phi_{t}^{h}\Phi_{-t_{n}}^{h}\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}z_{h,0}^{n}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}^{2}

\displaystyle=\bar{m}_{h}\left(z_{h,0}^{t},\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}z_{h,0}^{t}-\Phi_{t}^{h}\Phi_{-t_{n}}^{h}\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}z_{h,0}^{n}\right)

\displaystyle-\bar{m}_{h}\left(z_{h,0}^{n},\overline{\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}z_{h,0}^{t}-\Phi_{t}^{h}\Phi_{-t_{n}}^{h}\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}z_{h,0}^{n}}(t_{n})\right)

\displaystyle+a_{h}\left(\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}z_{h,0}^{n},\overline{\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}z_{h,0}^{t}-\Phi_{t}^{h}\Phi_{-t_{n}}^{h}\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}z_{h,0}^{n}}(t_{n})\right)

\displaystyle-a_{h}\left(\Phi_{t}^{h}\Phi_{t_{n}}^{h}\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}z_{h,0}^{n},\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}z_{h,0}^{t}-\Phi_{t}^{h}\Phi_{-t_{n}}^{h}\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}z_{h,0}^{n}\right),

where we have used the definition of
\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}
and introduced extra terms

\bar{m}_{h}\left(z_{h,0}^{n},\overline{\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}z_{h,0}^{t}-\Phi_{t}^{h}\Phi_{-t_{n}}^{h}\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}z_{h,0}^{n}}(t_{n})\right)\\
=a_{h}\left(\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}z_{h,0}^{n},\overline{\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}z_{h,0}^{t}-\Phi_{t}^{h}\Phi_{-t_{n}}^{h}\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}z_{h,0}^{n}}(t_{n})\right).

Next it is a straightforward analogue of (
5.7
) to see that, since
\Phi_{t}^{h}\Phi_{-t_{n}}^{h}z_{h}^{n}=\underline{z_{h}^{n}}(t)
,

\displaystyle\left|\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{h}^{n}}z_{h}^{n}-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}\Phi_{t}^{h}\Phi_{-t_{n}}^{h}z_{h}^{n}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\frac{1}{|\Gamma_{h}^{n}|}-\frac{1}{|\Gamma_{h}(t)|}\right|\left|\int_{\Gamma_{h}^{n}}z_{h}^{n}\right|+\frac{1}{|\Gamma_{h}(t)|}\left|\int_{\Gamma_{h}^{n}}z_{h}^{n}-\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}\Phi_{t}^{h}\Phi_{-t_{n}}^{h}z_{h}^{n}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\tau\|z_{h}^{n}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})},

where we have used that

\left|\frac{1}{|\Gamma_{h}^{n}|}-\frac{1}{|\Gamma_{h}(t)|}\right|\leq\left||\Gamma_{h}^{n}|-|\Gamma_{h}(t)|\right|\leq C\tau,

for the second inequality.
Hence one can observe that from (
5.9
)

\left|\bar{m}_{h}\left(z_{h,0}^{t},\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}z_{h,0}^{t}-\Phi_{t}^{h}\Phi_{-t_{n}}^{h}\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}z_{h,0}^{n}\right)-\bar{m}_{h}\left(z_{h,0}^{n},\overline{\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}z_{h,0}^{t}-\Phi_{t}^{h}\Phi_{-t_{n}}^{h}\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}z_{h,0}^{n}}(t_{n})\right)\right|\\
\leq C\tau\|z_{h}^{n}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}\left\|\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}z_{h,0}^{t}-\Phi_{t}^{h}\Phi_{-t_{n}}^{h}\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}z_{h,0}^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}.

By a similar argument, using (
5.8
), one also obtains

\left|a_{h}\left(\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}z_{h,0}^{n},\overline{\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}z_{h,0}^{t}-\Phi_{t}^{h}\Phi_{-t_{n}}^{h}\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}z_{h,0}^{n}}(t_{n})\right)-a_{h}\left(\Phi_{t}^{h}\Phi_{-t_{n}}^{h}\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}z_{h,0}^{n},\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}z_{h,0}^{t}-\Phi_{t}^{h}\Phi_{-t_{n}}^{h}\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}z_{h,0}^{n}\right)\right|\\
\leq C\tau\left\|z_{h,0}^{n}\right\|_{-h}\left\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}\left(\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}z_{h,0}^{t}-\Phi_{t}^{h}\Phi_{-t_{n}}^{h}\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}z_{h,0}^{n}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}.

Lastly we note that
\left\|z_{h,0}^{n}\right\|_{-h}\leq C\|{z_{h,0}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}\leq C\|{z_{h}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}
, and by using Poincaré’s inequality one finds

\displaystyle\left\|\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}z_{h,0}^{t}-\Phi_{t}^{h}\Phi_{-t_{n}}^{h}\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}z_{h,0}^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|\nabla_{\Gamma_{h}}\left(\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}z_{h,0}^{t}-\Phi_{t}^{h}\Phi_{-t_{n}}^{h}\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}z_{h,0}^{n}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}

\displaystyle+C\tau\|z_{h,0}^{n}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}^{n})}.

Here the rightmost term comes from the fact that

\left|\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}z_{h,0}^{t}-\Phi_{t}^{h}\Phi_{-t_{n}}^{h}\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}z_{h,0}^{n}\right|=\left|\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}\Phi_{t}^{h}\Phi_{-t_{n}}^{h}\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}z_{h,0}^{n}\right|\leq C\tau\left\|z_{h,0}^{n}\right\|_{-h}\leq C\tau\|z_{h,0}^{n}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{h}(t))}.

This first equality follows from the fact that
\int_{\Gamma_{h}(t)}\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}z_{h,0}^{t}=0
, and the subsequent inequalities follow from (
5.7
) and the Poincaré inequality along with the fact that
\int_{\Gamma_{h}^{n}}{\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{S_{h}}z_{h,0}^{n}}=0
.
The result now follows.
∎
